**Current application verification (2026-09-10):** build/vet and fresh documentation packages pass. The full application suite is not green on the later shared source. DOC48-07 records the reproducible fresh-schema `Store`/role-registry mismatch plus shared-fixture/time and server-order follow-up. Four mock-webhook failures caused by the capture delivery switch were resolved by the correct test environment; they are not product fixes.

**Stage 48 completion definition:** authoritative core rebuilt and approved; all living documents classified/owned/reviewable; user help canonical and executable; technical/operational/implementation/assurance/legal material separated by lifecycle; moves completed in recoverable batches; only eligible files deleted through explicit evidence gates; generated checks are pure and CI-enforced; documentation stays outside the production footprint except the bounded KB content intentionally served.

---

## Stage 49 — Evidence-driven maximum-security program ⏳ (planned 2026-09-03; ≈40-60 sessions, continuous release gate)

**North-star, not a marketing claim:** “most secure in the world” is not an objectively provable permanent state. The product goal is the strongest practical, independently evidenced security posture for each supported deployment and industry configuration. Never claim “unhackable,” universally secure, certified or compliant. Publish the exact scope, release, configuration, controls tested, residual risks, exceptions, assessor and expiry behind every assurance statement.

**Security constitution:** secure by default; deny and fail closed; least privilege; explicit tenant/entity/location/owner scope; server-authoritative business decisions; one durable outcome per command; no shared or hidden support access; no hard-coded/default credential; no secret or sensitive payload in source, URL, client storage, ordinary log or documentation; no control silently disabled to improve speed; no security-through-obscurity; no production claim without prevention, detection, response and recovery evidence. A security control that operators cannot understand, users route around, or the team cannot patch is not complete.

**Dependency and ownership rule:** Stage 49 does not reopen or duplicate work. **47.0-47.7** remain the P0 stop-ship remediation; **47.8** owns browser/session containment; **47.11** owns bounded request/worker/side-effect safety; **47.16** owns India-first control implementation; **47.17** owns reference-journey release evidence; **48.6** owns the normative security/privacy/legal documents; existing **20.5/26.11.1** own the external penetration-test engagement; **38.2d** owns OAuth2 client credentials. Stage 49 turns those controls into one threat-led security system and adds the missing platform, SDLC, detection, recovery and continuous-assurance gates. Security, privacy, infrastructure, product, finance/control, data, SRE and qualified legal/audit owners approve their domains; an AI/developer may draft and test but cannot self-certify them.

**Lightweight security rule:** retain one Go application plus PostgreSQL and the vanilla-JS client. No mandatory WAF appliance, service mesh, SIEM cluster, secrets platform, message broker, policy engine, antivirus daemon, Node toolchain or security sidecar merely to obtain a checkbox. Prefer compile-time route contracts, shared middleware, PostgreSQL constraints/transactions, OS/reverse-proxy controls, bounded structured events and optional export adapters. A new dependency/service requires 47.18.4 evidence and an ADR. Every 49.x item records binary/static-byte, cold-start, request CPU/latency, idle/peak RSS, DB/index/WAL/log/backup growth and operator burden. Security evidence obeys approved retention/legal holds and may not recreate the audit-log space failure Stage 47 found.

**Priority rule:** 49.S0 and the applicable Stage 47 P0 items are release-blocking. 49.S1-S3 must close before an internet-facing multi-tenant Production claim. 49.S4 must close before the corresponding enterprise, regulated or contractual assurance claim. Existing module work may continue only when it does not alter the same security boundary without updating its threat model and tests.

### Phase 49.S1 — Identity, authorization, application and data integrity

- [ ] **49.2 Identity, authentication, recovery and machine identity** — prevent account takeover without making safe recovery impossible. — *2026-09-08: 49.2.2 closed. 2026-09-09: 49.2.4 closed (see its own note). Parent stays open pending 49.2.1, 49.2.3, 49.2.5-49.2.8.*
  - [ ] **49.2.1 Identity lifecycle.** Unique human identity; no shared cashier/admin/support account; joiner/mover/leaver approval; immediate disable; session/service-token revocation; dormant-account expiry; authoritative HR/admin linkage and periodic access recertification.
  - [x] **49.2.2 Password baseline.** Long passphrases, breached/common-password rejection using a privacy-preserving bounded method, modern adaptive password hashing with version/cost migration, constant-time comparisons, no arbitrary periodic reset, no recoverable password and safe rate-limited change flow. — *2026-09-08: `engines/password_policy.go` + embedded `engines/common_passwords.txt` (~300 curated entries incl. India-corporate `Word@123` patterns) is the one choke point `handleChangePassword`, `CompletePasswordReset` and `handleCreateUser` all call now — length (new `security.password_min_length` setting, default 12, Stage 30.7 registry pattern), denylist match, username-equality and a cheap ascending/descending/all-same-character sequence check. **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed**: `handleLogin`'s bcrypt check had a plaintext fallback (`u.PasswordHash != req.Password`) left over from old local-seed configs — every real seed is already a bcrypt hash so it had no legitimate caller, but its failure mode was backwards for a security control (a `password_hash` value that was ever plaintext, from a bad migration or manual SQL fix, would authenticate via a non-constant-time `!=`); removed, bcrypt is now the only accepted form. Added bcrypt cost-migration (`handleLogin` re-hashes at `bcrypt.DefaultCost` on successful login if the stored hash's cost is lower — best-effort, does not bump credential_version since it's not a real credential change). "No arbitrary periodic reset" and "no recoverable password" were already true and are now fully true with the fallback gone. **Also closes the credential_version half of 49.2.4** — see that item.*

  - [x] **49.2.4 Recovery cannot bypass authentication.** Uniform responses, high-entropy short-lived single-use reset tokens stored hashed, request/verify attempt limits, session revocation after reset, risk notification, reauthentication for destination/MFA change and auditable helpdesk recovery with dual control for privileged users. — *2026-09-08: uniform responses, hashed single-use tokens, request/verify rate limits, session revocation after reset and risk notification all built (see prior note). 2026-09-09: the last two clauses closed. **Reauthentication for destination change** — `handleUpdateProfile` (`internal/server/handlers_profile.go`) previously changed the account's registered email with no reauthentication at all: a hijacked session (stolen bearer token, unattended workstation) could silently redirect "Forgot Password" to an attacker-controlled address. It now requires `current_password` (the same bcrypt check `handleChangePassword` already performs) whenever the email actually changes — a same-value resubmission from a form that always sends every field is exempt, so an idle-timeout-only save needs nothing. `engines.SendRecoveryEmailChangedNotice` (new, sibling to `SendPasswordChangedNotice`) alerts the OLD address, never the new one (a risk notice, not a confirmation flow — building email verification is out of scope here). MFA-change reauthentication was already true: the only self-service MFA mutation, `/me/mfa/reenroll`, already requires the account password (Stage 32.5); there is still no self-service "disable MFA" endpoint at all. **Auditable dual-control helpdesk recovery for privileged users** — the actual missing piece: there was no admin-driven "reset another user's password" endpoint at all before this. New `engines/admin_password_reset.go` (`RequestAdminPasswordReset`/`ExecuteApprovedPasswordResetRequest`) + new doctype `PasswordResetRequest` (`db/migrations_stage49_2_4_admin_password_reset.sql`, seeded into the existing generic maker-checker engine exactly like `HoldReleaseRequest`/`LoyaltyRedemptionRequest`) + new `POST /api/v1/admin/users/reset-password` (Users screen → **Reset Password**). An ordinary target is reset immediately, in one call, the same single-admin shape `handleAdminResetUserMFA` already uses for MFA — a one-time 20-character random password (same unambiguous alphabet as MFA recovery codes) is returned once, credential_version bumps (revoking every existing session), and `SendPasswordChangedNotice` fires. **A Super Admin target instead requires a second, different Super Admin to approve** via the existing generic `/api/v1/approval/submit`+`/decide` (maker-checker already refuses the same actor as both requester and approver); the reset does not execute until Approved, and the generated password is surfaced **only in the approver's own decide response** (`handleDecideApproval` gained one doctype branch, since this is the one approval hook in the codebase whose result may not reach the requester — every other doctype's hook lives inside `engines.DecideApproval` itself and returns nothing to the HTTP layer). A reason is mandatory once the target is privileged, optional otherwise. An admin cannot use this endpoint to reset their own password (points to My Profile instead). Every decision — immediate or approved — leaves a `PasswordResetRequest` evidence row alongside the audit log entry. **Real gap found and deliberately deferred, not silently accepted**: `handleAdminResetUserMFA` has the identical single-admin-clears-a-Super-Admin's-second-factor shape and was NOT retrofitted with dual control in this session — its own test (`TestAdminResetUserMFA`) seeds actor and target with the same privileged role by construction, so the fix is a real behavior change deserving its own session, not a drop-in.     *Built 2026-10-09 (five-item pass, item 3), to the scope the user set.*
    - **Where:** new `internal/server/request_envelope.go`, called once from `apiMiddleware`
      and from `publicAPIMiddleware` (the second caller boundary). It replaces the bare 2 MB
      `MaxBytesReader`.
    - **Limits** (in ADMIN_GUIDE §D's table): methods allowlisted (405 `GLOBAL-0303`). JSON or
      multipart only, plus per-route CSV/CSP types, and compressed bodies refused (415
      `GLOBAL-0304`). Bodies 2 MB, or 10/16/100/5 MB on the upload and import routes (413
      `GLOBAL-0305`). Headers 100 fields/32 KB (431 `GLOBAL-0306`). JSON is streamed and checked
      for depth 32, lists of 50,000, strings of 1 MB (2 MB for StickerTemplate), exact duplicate
      keys including escape-spelled ones, and trailing values (400 `GLOBAL-0307`). A 55 s
      deadline flows through `r.Context()`; an expired one answers 504 `GLOBAL-0308` instead of
      `writeEngineError`'s empty 499.
    - **Measured first:** stored documents reach depth 3, arrays of 7 and strings of 654. A
      sticker logo is ≤400 KB, about 546 KB as a data URL, and all of a design's logos share the
      one `design` string. `max_import_rows` is 20,000. Measuring found that the old 2 MB cap
      silently overrode every larger per-handler limit, because a nested `MaxBytesReader` cannot
      raise an outer one: media upload, imports and the hook were all really capped at 2 MB.
    - **Decisions:** case-variant keys are allowed, because `copies_override`, `dispositions` and
      `exchange_for` are SKU/line-keyed maps. A body with no `Content-Type` is read as JSON and
      fully bounded; the allowlist's real job is blocking preflight-free `text/plain`/form
      bodies. Rejections are not logged (log-flood risk).
    - **Tests:** `request_envelope_test.go` has one case per limit through `apiMiddleware`
      (status, code, matching correlation id, no leak). Large legitimate payloads pass: a
      3.0 MB 20,000-row multipart CSV, a 1.6 MB three-logo sticker design and a 2,000-line PO;
      the first two are refused on a default route. Plus deadline-vs-abandoned.
    - **Live on scratch :8147:**
      - a real three-logo StickerTemplate save returned 200;
      - a real 3.4 MB/20,000-row import preview passed the envelope;
      - dup key → 400, TRACE → 405, text/plain → 415, each with its correlation id;
      - a Playwright sweep of all 53 lazy views + 4 setup screens made 341 API calls with zero
        envelope rejections.

      **Still per row:** a row with no id/code draws its sequence number with one query. Every

      zero page errors. ADMIN_GUIDE §D limits table updated.
  - [ ] **49.4.5 Host/proxy/cache security.** Explicit trusted-proxy list; canonical host/tenant binding; reject spoofed forwarding headers and host ambiguity; HSTS only after correct TLS scope; no-store on session/sensitive responses; safe cache variation; clickjacking, MIME sniffing, referrer and browser-capability policies.
  - [ ] **49.4.6 Canonical input validation.** Normalize once, validate business and storage form, reject control/bidi/confusable/path characters where unsafe, parameterize values, allowlist identifiers/sort/filter operators and test parser differentials across proxy/Go/PostgreSQL/browser.
  - [ ] **49.4.7 API misuse controls.** Per-command idempotency and replay window, resource/tenant-aware pagination and export, optimistic/row locking as appropriate, ETag/version checks for dangerous stale writes, consistent 401/403/404 disclosure policy and bounded rate/concurrency limits.
  - [ ] **49.4.8 Browser/API security regression suite.** Stored/reflected/DOM XSS, CSRF, CORS, clickjacking, request smuggling ambiguity at the deployed proxy/app pair, method override, open redirect, header injection, cache poisoning, path traversal, SQL/filter injection, mass assignment and oversized/decompression payloads.
  - **Acceptance:** stolen script execution cannot read reusable session material; cross-site requests cannot perform an authorized mutation; malformed/oversized/ambiguous inputs stop at one shared boundary without resource exhaustion or information leakage; strict policy works in the declared browsers/devices.

- [ ] **49.5 Server-authoritative business controls, fraud resistance and invariant security** — security includes money, stock, tax, ownership and approvals, not only login.
  - [ ] **49.5.1 Command catalog** for sale, quote/discount/override, tender, loyalty, return/refund/exchange, allocation/pick/ship, adjustment/count, vendor/payment, journal/period, payroll, master merge, credential/config change and export. Name actor capability, authoritative inputs, state preconditions, invariants, idempotency and audit result.
  - [ ] **49.5.2 Close Stage 47 atomicity work.** **47.2-47.5** must prove server price/tax/cost, exactly-once sale/return, owner isolation and reconciliation. Apply the same transaction/idempotency/failure-injection analysis to every high-value command.
  - [ ] **49.5.3 Workflow transition enforcement.** State machines reject skipped/backward/duplicate transitions, direct generic-doctype mutations and concurrent stale approvals; cancellation/reversal creates a compensating event and never edits away history.
  - [ ] **49.5.4 Abuse/fraud patterns.** Detect threshold splitting, excessive void/refund/discount/manual price, rapid stock adjustment, negative-stock exception, bank/vendor change before payment, duplicate invoice/tender, period reopening, mass export and unusual privileged configuration changes; tune by role/tenant volume.
  - [ ] **49.5.5 Trusted totals and reconciliation.** Persist authoritative money/quantity/currency/UOM/tax/valuation versions; balance subledger↔GL, order↔shipment↔invoice↔payment, stock availability↔ledger↔ownership and command↔audit/outbox. Exceptions are owned, visible and cannot be dismissed without evidence.
  - [ ] **49.5.6 Safe overrides.** Named capability, bounded value/duration/scope, reason, step-up/approval where risk requires, prominent operator feedback, evidence and after-the-fact exception review. No boolean “force” field creates a general bypass.
  - [ ] **49.5.7 Adversarial invariant tests.** Tampered client, lost response, retry/replay, two clerks/tabs/devices, boundary amount splitting, clock/currency/UOM changes, partial failure/process kill, webhook duplication, malicious import and direct generic-API attempt.
  - **Acceptance:** an authorized insider or tampered client cannot create an unauthorized economic result merely by changing payload/order/timing/path; every high-value command yields zero or one complete reconciled outcome and an explainable evidence trail.

Drift-guarded (`TestSensitiveFieldCategoriesAreAllClassified`).

No payment PAN/CVV/PIN/track data found anywhere in the schema — **2026-09-16: no longer just a one-time grep**, `internal/securityscan/bypass.go`'s new `payment-card-data` category (47.16.5) makes this an executed regression that fails the build if it ever changes. A full field-by-field "does this still have a purpose" sweep across every doctype is not done.

CSPRNG generation, purpose separation, version/key ID, rotation, old-ciphertext migration and an operator re-encryption command (`tenantctl reencrypt-channel-credentials`) built and tested for the JWT signing key and channel-credential key. `[needs decision: dual-control key recovery — no named second key-custodian role exists]`; a live compromise drill is a process exercise, not something a session can certify.
  - [ ] **49.6.6 Secret lifecycle.** Startup redaction and one-time-display already existed. Found and fixed a real leak: `engines/password_reset.go` logged a full, working password-reset link in production on an SMTP send failure (risk register R-14, closed) — while reconciling Stage 49.7's parallel work, the same gap was also found and fixed in two more SMTP-sending functions, `SendRecoveryEmailChangedNotice`/`SendPasswordChangedNotice`. Full repo/history/build/backup leak-scanning beyond the existing `internal/securityscan` bypass scanner is not built.

Admin API added (`POST/GET /api/v1/admin/privacy/requests`, `.../decide`, `.../execute`, `POST /api/v1/admin/privacy/legal-hold`, Super Admin only). Any other subject doctype is an explicit error, not a silent no-op. Not done: sweeping search/index, background jobs, files, logs, audit and backups for a subject's data, and `correction`/`consent_withdraw` have no automated effect yet (`[needs decision: which Customer marketing/comms flag consent-withdrawal should clear]`).
  - **Acceptance:** partially met — keys rotate and old ciphertext still decrypts (tested); the Customer erasure/legal-hold path is reproducible (tested); the full-system leak/minimum-data guarantee across every table/log/backup is not yet provable end-to-end.

### Phase 49.S2 — Hardened runtime, integrations and software supply chain

*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [ ] **49.7 Database, host, network and deployment hardening** — secure the actual supported deployment, not an imaginary cloud architecture. — *2026-09-11: closed as reviewable artifacts + what's honestly verifiable from this tree; 49.7.2/49.7.3/49.7.8 need a real host to actually accept. commit 82f5517.*
  - [x] **49.7.1 Least-privilege identities and filesystem.** `deploy/postgres_harden.sql` splits the shared `erp` role into `erp_app`(DML)/`erp_migrate`(schema owner)/`erp_backup`(SELECT); verified end-to-end against an isolated scratch database, fully dropped afterward. `deploy/erp.service` narrows writable paths to three explicit directories (was: all of `/opt`). — *Backup/migrate identity split ready now; the server's own runtime identity is blocked — see 49.7.4 note.*
  - [ ] **49.7.2 Service sandbox.** `deploy/erp.service` gains capability/namespace/device/kernel isolation, safe umask, file/process limits — `[needs deployment: verify via \`systemd-analyze security erp.service\` and a full smoke test on a real host]`. `SystemCallFilter`/`MemoryDenyWriteExecute` documented, deliberately left disabled pending that verification.
  - [ ] **49.7.3 Network boundary.** `deploy/README.md` Part G has the exact operator commands (default-deny, DB non-public, direct-origin/alt-port, IPv4/IPv6 parity) — `[needs deployment: none of it is checkable without a real host]`.
  - [x] **49.7.4 PostgreSQL baseline.** `deploy/postgres_harden.sql` sets least grants/schema ownership/default privileges, per-role timeouts/connection caps, revokes public's default CREATE; README adds a version/extension/logging checklist. — *Found and fixed a real gap while testing: `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` doesn't cover a tenant schema created after hardening ran — `engines/saas.go`'s `ProvisionTenantSchema` now grants it explicitly (no-op pre-hardening).*
  - [x] **49.7.5 Migration safety/security.** New checksum column on `public.schema_migrations` (SHA-256, computed on apply/baseline, verified as SB-024 with honest first-observation backfill for the ~157 pre-existing rows — renumbered from SB-023 during merge once Stage 49.6 was found to have independently claimed that ID first); a static scanner proves zero destructive DDL exists across all migration files today, with a documented reviewed-exception pattern for if one is ever needed.
  - [x] **49.7.6 Environment isolation.** Found and fixed **four** real sandbox-tenant side-effect leaks, not just the one first found: `sendPasswordResetEmail` (password_reset.go) and — found while reconciling against Stage 49.2's parallel work during merge — `SendRecoveryEmailChangedNotice`/`SendPasswordChangedNotice` (same file) never checked `IsSandboxTenant`; `DispatchNotification` (notifications.go) had neither the sandbox check nor the `ExternalSideEffectsEnabled()` kill switch. All four fixed to match `engines/webhook.go`'s existing pattern, each proven with a real TCP/HTTP canary test.
  - [ ] **49.7.7 Patch and configuration cadence.** Version/extension check commands added to `deploy/README.md`; no automated inventory tool built this pass — `[needs decision: is a dedicated version-inventory tool worth building, or is the README checklist sufficient]`.
  - [ ] **49.7.8 Hardened-deployment test** — `[needs deployment: requires a clean host/image, not attempted from this tree]`.
  - **Acceptance:** partially met — backup/migrate compromise no longer reaches schema-modify/superuser; **runtime-process compromise still can** (two existing HTTP routes, `POST /api/v1/admin/tenant/provision` and `POST /api/v1/admin/sandbox-tenants`, need `CREATE SCHEMA` through the server's own DB connection — documented in `deploy/README.md` Part A2.5 and risk register R-07, contradicting `docs/security/README.md`'s prior claim that provisioning "has no HTTP surface"). Network-path and clean-rebuild claims are `[needs deployment]`.

- [ ] **49.8 Files, imports/exports, webhooks, connectors, jobs and outbound-side-effect security** — every external boundary is hostile and replayable.
  - [ ] **49.8.1 Upload/file policy.** Allowlisted purpose/type/extension/signature, randomized server name, path normalization, per-file/request/tenant quota, streaming size limit, archive depth/ratio/member limit, safe image/document handling, isolated storage/origin and download disposition. Reject traversal, polyglot and decompression/zip bombs.
  - [ ] **49.8.2 Import/export safety.** Stage/validate/preview/approve/reconcile as **47.13**; no formula injection in spreadsheet output, hidden sensitive columns, unauthorized link traversal, parser ambiguity or unlimited memory/temp/disk use; generated downloads are tenant/user-bound, short-lived and revocable.
  - [ ] **49.8.3 Inbound webhook contract.** Per-tenant secret/key, strong signature over exact raw body and timestamp, bounded skew/replay store, event identity/idempotency, content/body limit, schema/state validation, generic response, secret rotation overlap and quarantine—not immediate blind mutation.
  - [ ] **49.8.4 Outbound webhook security.** HTTPS and verified hostname, destination approval, DNS/IP re-resolution and private/link-local/loopback/metadata-network denial unless explicitly governed, redirect restriction, connect/read/total limits, response-size cap, signature, secret rotation, replay-safe delivery and redacted history. Test DNS rebinding/SSRF.
  - [ ] **49.8.5 Connector credential and scope.** Separate per tenant/channel/environment, minimum provider permission, never returned after creation, owner/last-use/expiry visible, rotation/revocation and disconnect cleanup. Provider errors/payloads cannot inject HTML, secrets or cross-tenant IDs into UI/logs.
  - [ ] **49.8.6 Job/outbox boundary.** Complete **38.6/47.11**: allowlisted job type/schema, explicit tenant+actor, bounded payload/result/attempt/runtime/concurrency, no serialized secret, lease/idempotency, poison/DLQ authorization, safe cancellation/replay and two-replica takeover proof.
  - [ ] **49.8.7 Egress and external-side-effect control.** Inventory every DNS/HTTP/SMTP/SMS/payment/courier/channel/printer destination; development/test default off; production allowlist or approval where practical; emergency tenant/global kill switch preserves authoritative pending state and reconciliation.
  - [ ] **49.8.8 Integration abuse lab.** Forged/stale/reordered/duplicated webhook, provider compromise, credential revocation mid-command, slowloris/large response, TLS/DNS failure, redirect to private IP, poison job, printer unavailable and provider says success while local commit fails.
  - **Acceptance:** external input cannot escape its tenant, parser, resource or workflow boundary; outbound calls cannot become a general network oracle; retries/provider ambiguity yield one reconciled business outcome; disabling an integration never requires deleting evidence.

### Phase 49.S3 — Adversarial verification, detection, incident response and resilience

- [ ] **49.10 Layered security verification and break-the-ERP program** — repeatedly test controls from attacker, insider, tenant and business-abuse viewpoints.
  - [x] **49.10.1 Version-pinned verification matrix** against the current adopted application/API verification standard, common weakness taxonomy, secure-development framework and deployment benchmarks. Map only applicable controls; exclusions need rationale and owner. — **built 2026-09-13**, `docs/security/verification-standards-matrix.md`: OWASP ASVS 5.0.0 (17 chapters) and CWE Top 25 (2025 edition) fetched live rather than recalled from training data, mapped chapter/weakness-by-weakness with a real citation or an explicit open Stage-item number for every row — never left blank. CIS benchmark target versions confirmed live against production (Ubuntu 22.04.5 LTS, PostgreSQL 14.24) via SSH; exact CIS document edition number not yet pinned, recorded honestly rather than guessed. Reuses NIST SP 800-218/SSDF (already adopted, Stage 48/49.9) rather than re-adopting a framework. Explicitly **not** a line-by-line ~350-requirement ASVS audit — that's real, separate follow-up work, named as such rather than faked. `go run ./cmd/doclint`/`go build ./...` clean.
  - [ ] **49.10.2 Unit/property/fuzz/race tests** at shared parsers, normalization, auth, tenant resolution, permission evaluation, state machines, money/quantity arithmetic, idempotency, signatures, archive/upload parsing and concurrent audit/job code. Every fixed security bug receives a durable regression.
  - [ ] **49.10.3 Full authorization/tenant harness** creates two hostile tenants with colliding human IDs/SKUs/names, every baseline role and worker/integration identity; probes routes, searches, counts, reports, links, attachments, exports, caches, jobs, logs and error/timing behavior.
  - [ ] **49.10.4 Authenticated application/API DAST** using isolated synthetic data and every privilege tier; test both deployed proxy and direct app boundary, WebSocket/SSE if introduced, and business workflows beyond scanner-default payloads.
  - [ ] **49.10.5 Resource-exhaustion tests** for slow clients, large/fragmented/compressed inputs, expensive filters/reports/exports, high-cardinality login/limiter keys, job/webhook retry storms, connection pool exhaustion, disk/WAL/log growth and browser/RF constrained memory.
  - [ ] **49.10.6 Insider/red-team scenarios.** Cashier payroll access, admin cross-tenant action, DBA audit tamper, support impersonation, malicious import/attachment, price/refund/threshold fraud, owner crossover, connector SSRF/secret theft, stolen backup, compromised CI artifact and destructive operator.
  - [ ] **49.10.7 Independent penetration test and retest.** Execute existing **20.5/26.11.1** scope only after internal stop-ship closure; include source-assisted architecture/business-logic/API/multi-tenant testing, deployed configuration and authenticated roles—not merely an anonymous scanner. Retest every critical/high fix.
  - [ ] **49.10.8 Finding discipline.** One register with reproducibility/evidence, affected releases/configurations/tenants, exploitability/impact, owner/SLA, fix/regression/retest and disclosure decision. Never close on code change alone or suppress by relabeling severity.
  - **Acceptance:** zero unresolved known critical/high finding in the claimed Production scope; every applicable control has automated or dated human evidence; independent retest and internal business-abuse exercises agree the supported trust boundaries hold.

- [ ] **49.11 Security events, tamper evidence, detection and response-ready observability** — detect meaningful attacks without logging the database twice.
  - [ ] **49.11.1 Event taxonomy.** Successful/failed auth and recovery, MFA/credential/session lifecycle, authorization denial, privilege/config/secret/integration change, sensitive read/export, approval/override, fraud/invariant exception, tenant-routing failure, webhook/job replay/DLQ, verifier failure and deployment/migration event.
  - [ ] **49.11.2 High-quality event schema.** Trusted time, tenant, actor/service/session pseudonymous ID, source/device/network as lawful, action/target/outcome/reason, capability/policy/software versions and correlation/command identity; never secret/full sensitive payload; distinguish attack from ordinary user validation.
  - [ ] **49.11.3 Complete Stage 47 audit design.** **47.7** provides transactional tamper evidence, checkpoints, bounded hot query, encrypted archive/legal hold and restore verification. Security events reference—not duplicate—business audit evidence.
  - [ ] **49.11.4 Actionable detections.** Password spray/credential stuffing, unusual privileged access, impossible tenant/owner scope, rapid grant/export/refund/void/adjustment, disabled-user activity, audit gap/tamper, webhook signature/replay surge, job poison/retry storm, backup/verifier failure and drift/new public exposure.
  - [ ] **49.11.5 Alert engineering.** Severity, owner, threshold/baseline, suppression/dedup window, evidence link and first response; page only actionable urgent events, queue lower-risk review, detect missing expected signal and regularly test delivery/escalation.
  - [ ] **49.11.6 Optional export adapter.** Bounded batch/checkpoint export to a customer-selected log/SIEM destination when required; backpressure cannot block the business transaction or cause unbounded local growth; export is signed/encrypted as appropriate and tenant/privacy scoped.
  - [ ] **49.11.7 Security operations views** reveal posture, expiring keys/certs/exceptions, privileged sessions, risky grants, failed verifiers/backups/jobs, incidents/findings and data growth to authorized operators only; no raw secret or universal cross-tenant dashboard for ordinary tenant admins.
  - [ ] **49.11.8 Detection drills** inject known events end-to-end and measure coverage, time-to-detect/triage/contain, evidence completeness, false positives and disk/CPU overhead; missing telemetry is a failed control, not a passing “no alerts” result.
  - **Acceptance:** each priority attack/abuse case has a tested detection and named response; audit/security hot data stays within the Stage 47 budget; loss/tamper/backpressure of the detection path is itself visible; ordinary business traffic remains smooth.

- [ ] **49.12 Incident response, forensic readiness, breach workflow and safe recovery** — assume prevention can fail.
  - [ ] **49.12.1 Incident taxonomy and authority.** Severity and decision tree for account/tenant/data/integrity/availability/supply-chain/provider/insider incidents; incident commander, technical/privacy/legal/business/customer roles, alternates and out-of-band contacts/access.
  - [ ] **49.12.2 One-page containment runbooks** for stolen password/session/API key, privileged account, tenant crossover, malicious connector/webhook, exposed secret, vulnerable dependency/release, ransomware/destructive DB action, audit failure and data exfiltration. Preserve evidence while stopping harm.
  - [ ] **49.12.3 Emergency controls.** Revoke user/session/client/key; freeze high-risk capability/tenant/integration/job/export; rotate secret/key; block artifact/version; enter safe read-only/degraded mode where designed. Controls require strong operator auth, reason, audit and tested recovery; no undocumented kill command.
  - [ ] **49.12.4 Forensic readiness.** Trusted synchronized time, release/config/migration hashes, bounded relevant logs/audit/checkpoints, access records and chain-of-custody collection. Evidence export is tenant/time scoped, encrypted, integrity-manifested and does not alter originals.
  - [ ] **49.12.5 Notification decision workflow.** Versioned jurisdiction/contract/customer/processor matrix, discovery/assessment clock, qualified legal/privacy approval and evidence. CERT-In/DPDP/contractual actions align with **47.16**; the system does not make autonomous legal conclusions.
  - [ ] **49.12.6 Eradication and recovery.** Identify entry/root cause/blast radius/persistence; patch/rebuild from trusted signed artifact, rotate affected credentials, restore/reconcile, verify tenant/control integrity, monitor recurrence and avoid reconnecting a compromised worker/integration blindly.
  - [ ] **49.12.7 Post-incident learning.** Blameless timeline, control/test/detection/runbook/doc/support changes, customer commitments and tracked completion. Preserve immutable incident record; sanitize reusable lessons without erasing evidence.
  - [ ] **49.12.8 Tabletop and live drills.** Quarterly priority tabletop plus scheduled technical credential-revocation, malicious-release rollback, tenant-isolation, backup/restore and provider-outage drills; measure legal decision, detection, containment, RTO/RPO and communication accuracy.
  - **Acceptance:** the on-call operator can contain each priority scenario without developer invention or cross-tenant collateral damage; required decision/evidence clocks are met in drills; recovery uses a verified trusted state and every money/stock/security invariant reconciles afterward.

- [ ] **49.13 Availability, abuse resistance, backup integrity and ransomware resilience** — secure service remains usable for legitimate work.
  - [ ] **49.13.1 Availability threat/SLO model** per login, command finalization, lookup/report/export, worker/integration and restore path; identify resource pools and define which work sheds, queues, degrades or remains reserved under pressure.
  - [ ] **49.13.2 Bounded admission control.** Complete **47.11** for request/body/query/concurrency/rate/job limits with bounded memory and useful `Retry-After`; isolate/reserve capacity for auth, health, command completion, cancellation and incident controls. Limits cannot be bypassed with unique keys or used for cheap victim lockout.
  - [ ] **49.13.3 Query/storage safety.** Timeouts, pagination, result/temporary-file quotas, worker concurrency, connection pool, WAL/disk thresholds and cancellation for every report/search/export/import/audit path; expensive operations become authorized bounded jobs, never an unbounded goroutine.
  - [ ] **49.13.4 Backup threat model.** 3-2-1-style risk separation appropriate to deployment, encrypted off-host/off-account or immutable copy, least-privilege backup vs restore credentials, monitored success/size/age, integrity manifest, retention/legal hold and deletion protection.
  - [ ] **49.13.5 Restore is a security control.** Automated isolated restore verification, malware/compromise point selection, key availability, schema/release compatibility and post-restore tenant/money/stock/audit reconciliation. Backup success without a successful restore drill is failure.
  - [ ] **49.13.6 Ransomware/destructive-admin drill.** Assume application host/database credential or cloud admin is compromised; contain, preserve evidence, rebuild clean infrastructure, restore an uncompromised point, rotate trust and quantify data loss/outage against RPO/RTO.
  - [ ] **49.13.7 Graceful dependency failure.** DNS/TLS/DB/storage/payment/courier/channel/email/SMS/printer/clock/disk-full and backup-target failures produce explicit safe state, bounded retry and reconciliation; never silently skip security evidence or accept client truth because a dependency is down.
  - **Acceptance:** hostile or accidental load cannot starve priority commands or grow RAM/disk without bound; recovery meets approved RTO/RPO and proves integrity; no single online administrator/credential/failure domain can both destroy production and every usable backup.

  - [ ] **49.17.1 Per-change security gate.** Surface/threat/data/capability/dependency/config impact; tests and code review; migration/rollback; docs/runbook; resource delta. High-risk “not applicable” requires security-owner rationale.
  - [ ] **49.17.2 Release-candidate gate.** Exact signed commit/artifact/SBOM/migrations/config; clean build/vet/tests; route/permission/surface diff; secret/dependency/static/dynamic/fuzz/race results appropriate to risk; backup/restore and rollback; zero unresolved critical/high; approved expiring lower-risk exceptions.
  - [ ] **49.17.3 Configuration go-live gate.** Supported capability level, hardened deployment/drift result, tenant role/SoD review, credential/key/cert ownership and rotation, external destinations, backup/RTO/RPO drill, incident contacts, data/retention/legal basis, device/integration proof and customer acceptance.
  - [ ] **49.17.4 Security scorecard with evidence, not one magic number.** Attack-surface classification, privileged/access recertification, tenant negative-test coverage, unresolved risk age, patch/rotation/cert age, control/detection/drill pass rate, restore/RTO/RPO, dependency provenance, data/audit growth and user-reported security friction.
  - [ ] **49.17.5 Fixed operating cadence.** Continuous critical advisory/intake; per-release surface/control evidence; monthly vulnerability/patch/access/detection/data-growth review; quarterly threat model/access/vendor/tabletop/restore sampling; annual independent pentest and broader program review or faster when risk/contract requires.
  - [ ] **49.17.6 Canary and rollback.** Deploy security changes by risk-aware canary/config scope; monitor auth denial, business invariant, latency/error, worker/backlog and support friction; rollback never restores a known vulnerability without explicit emergency containment and executive/security decision.
  - [ ] **49.17.7 Independent challenge.** Rotate internal attacker/persona reviews and periodically use qualified external specialists for application/API/multi-tenancy, cloud/host, finance/fraud, privacy/legal, RF/device and supply-chain domains; evidence expires and must be renewed after material change.
  - [ ] **49.17.8 Honest disclosure and improvement.** Publish supported version/configuration, security contact, material known limits and dated assurance scope; feed incidents, near misses, support workarounds, denied-user friction, performance regressions and assessor findings back into threat model, requirements and regression tests.
  - **Acceptance:** no release/configuration reaches Production without reproducible security evidence and recoverability; overdue high risk, expired assessment/exception, failed restore/verifier/detection or unknown surface automatically lowers/blocks the affected capability status; operators can reproduce the posture from a clean checkout and approved deployment record.

- [ ] **49.18 Security weight, performance and storage budget ledger** — maximum security must remain ultra-light and butter-smooth.
  - [ ] **49.18.1 Per-control before/after measurement:** binary/static bytes, startup, idle/peak RSS, auth and normal-request p50/p95/p99 CPU/latency, DB query/index/WAL, security/audit/log bytes per 1k commands, backup size/time, browser work and operational minutes/alerts.
  - [ ] **49.18.2 Bounded state everywhere.** Login/rate/session/revocation/CSRF/replay/idempotency/signature/job/alert/export/support-access records have key cardinality, TTL/retention, maximum payload, cleanup job, legal-hold behavior, index/query SLO and overload response.
  - [ ] **49.18.3 One-event/one-evidence design.** Reuse correlation and references between business audit, security event, job/outbox and incident evidence rather than copying full payload/diff four times; compress/archive only after integrity and retrieval proof.
  - [ ] **49.18.4 No client bloat.** Security UI uses native browser/platform capabilities and lazy-loaded existing modules; no remote font/script, general analytics SDK, CAPTCHA bundle or heavy crypto in the core path without measured threat benefit and privacy/accessibility/performance review.
  - [ ] **49.18.5 No infrastructure reflex.** First fix root cause in authorization/transactions/query bounds/config. CDN/WAF/managed secrets/SIEM/EDR may be optional deployment defenses when threat/contract warrants, but app security and a supported lightweight baseline cannot depend on buying them.
  - [ ] **49.18.6 Security performance cannot be “optimized away.”** Cache only safe versioned allow decisions/data, never across tenant/user/scope/state; do not skip password cost, authorization, signature, audit or encryption. Optimize shared parsing/query/index/serialization and measure attacker as well as legitimate cost.
  - [ ] **49.18.7 Multi-year forecast and cleanup drill** for sessions/events/audit/replay/jobs/findings/evidence/backups/archives by tenant size; demonstrate expiration/archive/legal hold/restore without table lock, disk exhaustion, lost investigation ability or production service addition.
  - **Acceptance:** Stage 47 latency/RSS/binary/browser/hot-data budgets still pass under security-enabled representative load; every security store/process is bounded and recoverable; the minimum secure production topology remains Go + PostgreSQL + existing proxy/OS facilities unless an approved evidence-based exception says otherwise.

**Stage 49 completion definition:** all supported surfaces, crown jewels and trust boundaries are owned and threat-modeled; Stage 47 stop-ships are closed or unsupported configurations are technically impossible; identity/session/authorization/tenant/business invariants withstand the adversarial matrix; data, keys, host, database, integrations, build and artifacts have verified lifecycle controls; priority attacks are detectable and rehearsed through containment/recovery; independent testing has no unresolved critical/high finding in scope; every customer/legal claim is configuration-specific and approved; continuous gates prevent silent regression; and the fully secured reference deployment remains within the lightweight performance, memory, storage and dependency budgets.

BLD-046 itself still stays open — this only resolved the mechanical manifest-drift blocker, not the item's own real, unaddressed "interrupted cases" gap (abandoned-request cancellation) described above.* — *2026-10-02: BLD-041 (lazy screen code and initial payload budgets) opened — real progress, stays open; first session to touch it. **Baseline measured** (3 real cold-cache HTTP runs against a disposable scratch server, `Accept-Encoding: gzip`, consistent across all 3): cold-core (index.html + styles.css + db.js + components/erp-typeahead.js + app.js + qz-print.js) = 347,586 bytes (~339.4 KiB) vs. the 180 KiB budget; initial JS alone = 307,602 bytes (~300.4 KiB) vs. the 120 KiB budget — app.js alone is ~96.7% of the JS total and the only real lever. **Approach decided**: native lazy loading via a plain dynamically-injected `<script src="...">` per view group, NOT an ES-module/`import()` rewrite — app.js stays one classic script, so every function/let/const a chunk declares at its top level becomes global exactly as it would if it still lived in app.js (classic `<script>` tags on one page share one global function/var and top-level `let`/`const` environment), meaning zero import/export wiring at any of its ~600 existing cross-references. New shared `loadViewChunk(src)` helper (dedup `Set` + one `<script>` element + onload/onerror) lets `renderViewContent`'s existing per-view dispatch branches `await` a chunk before calling its render function — fits the already-existing scratch-buffer-then-atomic-swap rendering pipeline with no changes to it, and a load failure renders the same `renderErrorPanel`-with-retry every other load failure in this app uses (not a crash, not silently skipped). **Mechanism proven end-to-end on 4 real, independently-verified view groups**, each confirmed self-contained first by grepping every identifier it declares across the whole ~24,500-line file (zero inbound references other than the one dispatcher call, zero outbound references to anything but shared/core helpers) before moving it: Manufacturing (`view-manufacturing.js`, ~570 lines, incl. its own `currentMfgTab`/`MFG_TABS` module state), Expenses (`view-expenses.js`), Fixed Assets (`view-assets.js`), and Stock Transfer (`view-transfers.js`, incl. its own `transferLineItems` state and its reads/writes of the shared core `state.docData` scratch array). Live-verified with Playwright for all 4: a cold load that never visits any of the four fetches 0 bytes of any of them; the first visit to each fetches its chunk exactly once; a second visit (even after navigating away) is served from cache with no re-fetch; every screen's tabs/actions/sub-views render and respond correctly (incl. Manufacturing's MRP tab and its Quality tab's delegation into the shared, still-core `renderDocTableView`); a simulated chunk-load failure on a fresh page (`route.abort`) shows the retry panel with zero uncaught page errors, and a subsequent real load succeeds. **Measured result**: cold-core dropped 347,586 → 337,701 bytes (~9.9 KB, ~2.8%) and initial JS 307,602 → 297,717 bytes across these 4 of ~56 total dispatched views — a small but real, fully mechanical, fully verified dent; closing the budget gap needs this same proven pattern applied to most of the remaining ~52 views, which is real, scoped, repeatable follow-up (same shape as the `guardAgainstDoubleSubmit` rollout's own 3-session mechanical scale-out), not a one-session undertaking. `node --check`/`go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (after two `attack_surface.json` regenerations for the new static files — `go run ./cmd/surfacescan -out %TEMP%\...` then copied in via PowerShell, no permission block this time); `graphify update .` and `pwsh docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` run after (the latter needed one `brain.map.json` edit: added a `public/view-*.js` glob to the existing "SPA Shell" region so future chunk files auto-claim without a further edit). Files touched: `public/app.js`, four new `public/view-*.js` chunk files, `docs/security/attack_surface.json`, `docs/brain/{brain.map.json,BRAIN.md,brain.html}`, `docs/generated/brain-manifest.json`. ## Stage 52 — Category-based sticker/label printing module (opened + built 2026-09-27)

User asked for a real label-printing module for a jewellery retailer: different categories need visually different labels (an earring tag looks nothing like a necklace tag), printing should start from an actual transaction (a GRN or Transfer Order) rather than manual SKU entry, whole-document or one-line-at-a-time, and the user wants full self-service control over what a label looks like via a drag-and-drop designer rather than a fixed preset. Entered Plan mode given the scope (new doctype, generic cross-doctype transaction loader, a from-scratch drag/resize canvas UI, generalized ZPL rendering); plan approved 2026-09-27.

*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

plus 4 new "How do I…?" rows, a §7A pointer in the §3 module map, a cross-ref from §6 step 9 (GRN), and a **Sticker Template** glossary row. **`docs/guides/ADMIN_GUIDE.md`**: new **§B.3.4 "Category-based sticker templates"** (the no-templates-means-identical-output backward-compat guarantee, the HR-Admin/Store-Manager/Cashier permission matrix, the 3-step resolution order, three pitfalls to warn users about — `Item.category` is unvalidated free text, `Inactive` is the retire switch, the default flag is effectively single-occupancy since the resolver has no `ORDER BY` — mm/DPI sizing incl. the 300dpi-printer trap, the required-Printer-record exception, the `sticker_print_log`/`print_job_log` split, and 52.7 as the documented extension point). **Three doc drifts found and fixed while verifying every claim against source**: (a) ADMIN_GUIDE's printing bullet claimed "Nothing depends on this being set up" for all print screens — untrue for Sticker Printing, which hard-requires a Printer record even on the browser-fallback path (`view-printing.js:401`), now scoped to QZ Tray with the exception called out; (b) both guides initially said the Printer needs **Default For** = `Sticker` — it does not, `resolveQZPrinter` honours an explicit `printer_code` first, so that wording was corrected rather than shipped; (c) `QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md`'s field-palette list was missing **Mfg Date** (present in `STICKER_DESIGNER_FIELDS`), now added, along with a Status note and cross-links to the two new sections. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./engines/...`, `go run ./cmd/doclint -root .` (320 files, 0 findings), `go test ./cmd/doclint/... ./internal/kb/...` and the Stage 52 engine tests are all clean.

So for a batch-tracked GRN: the two rows shared one checkbox and one copies value (unticking or editing either hit both), and the per-row **Print** button printed *both* lots, directly contradicting "print one line". 52.5's tests covered the resolver's lot-splitting but never selected one of two same-SKU lines, so nothing caught it. **Fixed at the one choke point both print paths already run through** rather than at each call site: new `engines.StickerLineSelection{SKU, BatchNo, Copies}` replaces the old `selectedSKUs []string` + `copyOverrides map[string]int` parameter pair on `PrintStickersForDocument`; `filterStickerLines` matches a line on SKU *and* lot (empty `BatchNo` = every lot of that SKU) and applies each selection's own copy count, so copy resolution now happens in exactly one place instead of two. `engines.SKUSelections` adapts the old shape, and a new shared `stickerSelections` helper in `internal/server` resolves both endpoints' requests identically so the browser-fallback and QZ-silent paths can never disagree. **Backward-compatible**: both endpoints accept a new optional `lines: [{sku, batch_no, copies}]` array *or* the original `skus`/`copies_override` fields, and a SKU-only selection still means "every lot of this SKU" — the manual scan flow and any existing API client are untouched. Frontend (`public/view-printing.js`) now keys row state by line index rather than SKU, sends the per-line shape, and shows a **Batch/Lot** column whenever any line on the document carries a lot (it stays hidden otherwise, so the common non-batch case looks exactly as before); `qzTryPrint` in `public/app.js` forwards the new field. New regression test `TestPrintStickersForDocumentSelectsOneLotOfASKU` pins all four behaviours (both lots by default, one lot addressed alone, a copy override hitting only its own lot, and a SKU-only selection still covering both). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` on the touched files, `node --check` on both JS files, the full Stage 52 engine suite are clean. No new route and no new migration, so `attack_surface.json` needs no regeneration (`TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` passed mid-session and later drifted only on a concurrent session's new Stage 53 migration file). **Live-verified over real HTTP** on a scratch instance (port 8097, throwaway two-lot GRN): per-line selection returned exactly the requested lot at the right qty, a per-lot copy override hit only its own lot, and both the legacy `skus` and no-selection forms still returned both lots unchanged; the QZ endpoint emitted 3 ZPL labels for a 3-copy single-lot run and 10 for the whole document. Fixtures and their log rows removed afterwards.

ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS batch_no TEXT` migration, one insert column, one extra column on the Print tab's history table.
