## Stage 53 — POS rebuild: standard retail till (opened 2026-10-04 from the user's live POS report)

User, looking at the live POS screen, rejected it outright: *"this is shitty pos. who ask cust code. Make it std pos, research and make it best / it should work with name search / Bill should which loction and store / location of store sellable non saleable / it should freeze untill i reset there should reset button"*.

**What is actually wrong today** (read from `public/view-pos.js`, `engines/qz_payload.go`, `engines/inventory.go` before planning, not assumed):
- The customer field is literally labelled **"Customer Code (optional)"** placeholdered *"For loyalty points"*. A `Customer` typeahead *is* already attached (`attachLinkTypeahead`), and the server's `?q=` search already scans **every** field (so name and phone already match) — but the box shows and stores the raw code, so it reads to a cashier as "type a code you don't know". The two loyalty buttons then re-read that box and say *"Enter a customer code first."* The capability is there; the UX denies it.
- The SKU box is `Scan or Enter SKU` with an `Item` typeahead, but `addSKUToPOSCart` sends **whatever string is in the box** straight to `/api/v1/availability?sku=`. A typed product *name* produces a zero-availability phantom line, not an error — there is no barcode→SKU resolution and no result list.
- `GetAvailableToSell` already returns `qc_hold`, `damaged`, `blocked` and `hold_qty` alongside `ats`; the cart throws all of it away and prints one "Available" number, so nothing on screen distinguishes sellable from non-sellable stock.
- `Location` (`db/migrations_stage17h_location_masters.sql`) has `type` (Store/Warehouse/HO) but **no sellable concept**. Nothing stops a POS session being opened, or a sale rung up, against a warehouse or HO. The screenshot the user sent is exactly this: a till sitting on **HO**.
- Receipts print the location **code** and nothing else — no store name, no legal entity, no GSTIN, no cashier. True on both paths: the browser fallback (`printPOSReceipt`, `${location}`) and the server-built ESC-POS payload (`BuildReceiptPayload`, `b.WriteString(location + "\n")`).
- Nothing freezes. The location picker is editable mid-shift, and after `Complete Sale` the cart is silently emptied behind a confirm dialog — the next customer's scan lands on a live, indistinguishable cart with no record of the bill just closed on screen.

**Two readings were ambiguous and were put to the user before planning (answered 2026-10-04): "freeze" = BOTH (lock the store for the shift AND freeze the till on the completed bill), "sellable/non-sellable" = BOTH (a flag on the `Location` master AND splitting the cart's availability figure).**

Scope discipline per this repo's first principle: no new framework, no new dependency, no new picker pattern. Customer/product search reuses `attachCodeNamePicker`/`attachLinkTypeahead` and the existing `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}?q=` endpoint; the sellable/non-sellable split needs **no backend change at all** (the fields are already in the availability response); the `Location.sellable` flag is one additive migration in the Stage 17.9 style.

- [x] **53.1 `Location.sellable` — the master flag.** `db/migrations_stage53_1_location_sellable.sql`: `Location` gains `sellable` (Select `Yes,No`, display_order 6), additive and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` in the Stage 17.9 style. **Scope decision — `mandatory = FALSE`, not TRUE as planned**: making it mandatory would reject a save of every pre-existing Location the next time anyone edited one, which is the "destructive rework" the first principle rules out. The gap that leaves (a Location created later with no value) is closed instead by a save-time stamp — see 53.1b. **Backfill rule changed after running it against the real dev DB, and this is the important finding of the whole Stage**: the planned "`Store` → Yes, everything else → No" rule produced **0 sellable locations out of 112**, because `migrations_stage17h_location_masters.sql` seeds every location code already in use with a *hardcoded* `type = 'Warehouse'`. Shipping that would not have been an additive change, it would have been an outage — every till in the tenant refusing every sale. The rule now has a first clause: a location with an existing `POSCart` or `POSSession` against it is marked `Yes` regardless of type, because what the business actually did is better evidence than a type nobody ever set. Re-run against the dev DB: 2 sellable (`HO`, `WH01` — the two with real POS history), 110 not. Verified by direct `psql` query before and after.
- [x] **53.1b Save-time stamp (added, not in the original plan).** `engines/master_data_validation.go` gains a `Location` case stamping `sellable` from `type` when blank (`Store` → Yes, else No), never overwriting an explicit value. Needed because 53.1 keeps the field optional: without it, a Location created after the migration would carry no value, and the 53.3 picker filter (`sellable=Yes`) would then disagree with what the server accepts — a picker hiding a location checkout would allow, or offering one it would refuse. Attached at `ValidateMasterDataRules`, the choke point every Location write already runs through.
- [x] **53.2 Enforce sellable server-side, at the choke point.** New `engines.LocationIsSellable` + `ValidatePOSSellableLocation` in `engines/location_masters.go`, returning a `*ValidationError` coded **`POSOFF-0245`** (registered in `internal/server/error_catalog_extensions.go`, HTTP 422, Blocking). Resolution order: no `Location` record → **permitted** (location is still free text on most doctypes per 17.9's own decision, so unregistered codes must keep working); explicit `Yes`/`No` → honoured; blank → derived from `type`. Called from **two** entry points: `OpenPOSSession` (engine-side, so a future third POS entry point inherits it) and `handleCheckout` (above the quote and every other side effect). Checkout is checked separately from session-open deliberately — an offline-queued sale replays through checkout *without re-opening a session*, and a Location can be flipped to `No` while a session is still open.
- [x] **53.3 POS location picker offers only sellable locations.** New generic `opts.filters` on `attachTypeahead` (`public/app.js`) — plain `field=value` pairs appended to the existing `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}` call, which already treats unknown query params as data filters. Added once at the shared control rather than as a POS-specific picker, so "Active vendors only" etc. is one line away for any screen. **Off by default — all ~45 existing pickers send the identical request they sent before.** `attachCodeNamePicker` makes two lookups of its own (resolve-on-blur, seed-the-name) and both had to be threaded with the same filter, or typing `HO` and tabbing away would have committed the exact location the filter exists to exclude. The generic Location picker on the other ~15 screens is untouched — a warehouse is still a valid transfer destination.
- [x] **53.4 Customer by name, not by code.** `Customer Code (optional)` / *"For loyalty points"* is gone. Replaced with a split display/hidden `attachCodeNamePicker(..., 'Customer')`: the cashier sees and searches the **name** (phone and code match too — the server's `?q=` scans every field via `jsonb_each_text`), `#pos-customer` still carries the code, so `checkout`, `redeem_points` and both loyalty buttons needed no change. Label **Customer (optional)**, placeholder *"Search by name or phone"*, and a live note underneath reading **"Walk-in — no customer attached"** until one is picked. Both loyalty buttons' *"Enter a customer code first."* re-worded.
- [x] **53.5 Quick-add a customer from the till.** `+ New Customer` → name + phone → `POST /api/v1/doc/Customer` → selected straight into the picker. Two fields because that is what a counter can actually get; the rest of the master is fillable later.
- [x] **53.6 Scan-or-search product entry.** `addSKUToPOSCart` no longer sends the raw box contents to `/api/v1/availability`. Resolution order: exact `Item.code` → exact `Item.barcode` → name search, all off **one** `?q=` request (the exact-match test runs on the returned rows, so the scanner path costs no extra round trip and never opens a list). Single match auto-adds; multiple matches render a keyboard-navigable result list (↑/↓/Enter/Esc) showing name, code and barcode; **no match now says so** instead of the old silent zero-availability phantom line. Both paths funnel through one `addResolvedItemToPOSCart`, so a scanned line and a searched line are identical.
- [x] **53.7 Cart shows sellable vs non-sellable availability.** New `posAvailabilityCell`: `ats` as *"N sellable"*, plus *"N not sellable (QC hold / damaged / blocked)"* only when `qc_hold + damaged + blocked + hold_qty > 0`. Frontend-only — `GetAvailableToSell` has always returned all four and the screen discarded them. A line whose qty exceeds sellable availability turns red at entry with *"Only N can be sold here"*, rather than being discovered by the server at checkout.
- [x] **53.8 Cart line controls.** `−` / `+` steppers either side of the qty box (box kept for larger numbers); `−` at qty 1 removes the line. The Item column now leads with the product **name** and shows the code underneath. Keyboard: Enter commits the scan box, arrows walk the result list.
- [x] **53.9 Terminal binding.** `applyPOSTerminalLock` called from `refreshPOSSessionStatus` (the one function that knows whether a session is open) and after the first cart line. Store box goes read-only with *"Bound to … for this session"*. **Reset Terminal** confirms, names how many cart lines will be lost, and clears them — and if a session is still open it clears the cart but **keeps the binding**, telling the cashier to close the session first: a session is a cash-accountability record, so Reset must not be a way to walk away from one.
- [x] **53.10 Post-sale freeze.** New `freezePOSTill` / `startNewPOSSale`. All three terminal outcomes funnel through one function — **paid**, **pending_approval** (orange, no receipt offered, explicit "do not hand over the goods"), **queued offline** (explicit "do not ring it up again") — so none can leave a half-live cart. The 409 `price_changed` path is deliberately *not* terminal; it re-enters checkout and freezes on whatever that returns. `New Sale (Reset)` clears cart/customer/coupon/discount/tender but **keeps the store binding** (re-picking the shop between customers would undo 53.9).
- [x] **53.11 Cash tendered and change due.** Cash-only tendered box computing **Change due** / **Short by** live, carried onto the frozen bill. Display-only and never sent to the server — treating a mistyped tender as anything more would turn it into a pricing bug. The frozen bill's change figure is computed against the server's `amount_due`, not the screen's pre-checkout total, so a server-side reprice cannot skew it.
- [x] **53.12 The bill says which store.** New `engines.ReceiptStoreHeader` / `ReceiptStore.Line()` resolving Location → name, and Location → `legal_entity` → LegalEntity → name/GSTIN/state. Wired into **both** server-side branches of `BuildReceiptPayload` (ESC-POS *and* the HTML driver fallback) and into the browser's `printPOSReceipt`. Cashier added: `storedPayload["cashier"]` is now persisted on the POSCart from the resolved identity, so the server can rebuild the full header from the cart alone. Deliberately forgiving — a missing Location, a Location with no entity, or an entity with no GSTIN each drop that line rather than failing the print, because a receipt must always print. `ReceiptStore.Line()` also de-duplicates the Stage 17.9 seed case where `name == code` (no more "HO (HO)").
- [x] **53.12b Pre-existing receipt bug found and fixed (not reported, not in the plan).** The **browser-fallback receipt printed `0.00` on every line**. Stage 47.2 made the checkout payload carry `sale_price: 0` for every server-priced line (only an unpriced assisted-mode line sends a figure), and `printPOSReceipt` was handed that same array — so `qty * sale_price` was 0 for every ordinary line and the line totals summed to nothing. It survived unnoticed because the QZ/ESC-POS path rebuilds the receipt server-side from the stored cart, so the fallback only prints in shops with no receipt printer configured. Fixed via a new `posReceiptItems()` that pairs each payload line back to its cart line and takes the resolved price (and the product name) from there.
- [x] **53.13 Tests.** New `engines/pos_sellable_location_stage53_test.go` — 20 subtests across four functions: `LocationIsSellable`'s full resolution order (including the two that would be easiest to get backwards: the **unregistered-code-still-sells** case and the **blank-on-a-Warehouse** case), the `POSOFF-0245` coded refusal, `OpenPOSSession` refusing a non-sellable location **and leaving no POSSession row behind**, `ReceiptStoreHeader`'s full/no-entity/name-equals-code/unregistered cases, and the 53.1b stamp. All pass. `node --check` clean on both JS files. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./engines/`, `gofmt` clean. Existing `engines/qz_receipt_invoice_test.go` still green after the header change.
- [x] **53.14 Guide docs, walked not summarised.** `USER_GUIDE.md` §4 rewritten end-to-end against the real new UI: prerequisites box now leads with **Sellable = Yes** (was "Type = Store", which is no longer what gates it), §4.0 covers the store lock and why a shop might be missing from the list, §4.1 walks scan-vs-search, the sellable/non-sellable figure, name/phone customer search, quick-add and cash tendered, **new §4.1a** explains the freeze and all three frozen outcomes, §4.3 rewritten around the Print Receipt button and what the header now carries, **new §4.4** (price-changed confirm) and **new §4.5** (Reset Terminal, including the session-still-open case). §4.2/§4.3 physically reordered into sequence (numbers and all cross-references unchanged). 8 new "How do I…?" rows. §13's worked example Steps 7-8 rewritten (they still described the old flow). `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` gains **§B.3.0.4** (what `sellable` gates in all three places, the full backfill rule *including the 17.9 all-Warehouse trap*, the unregistered-code pass-through, the blank-is-stamped behaviour, and an explicit "check this after the migration, do not assume it" warning) and **§B.3.0.5** (what the receipt header is built from, and that filling in LegalEntity is what makes it tax-compliant). `go run ./cmd/doclint -root .` — 322 files, 4 findings, **none in the files this Stage touched**.
- [x] **53.17 A real Reset button, separate from Refresh (user follow-up, 2026-10-04).** User, on seeing 53.9/53.10: *"There should be button of reset. instead of refresh. Refresh will have cahe and have all info but reset button will reset all."* The header's one button was labelled **Sync**, titled *"Refresh local data store"*, and re-fetched labels + registered doctypes only — every cache survived it, which is correct for "a label changed" and useless for "this screen is wrong". **Built as two buttons, not one stronger one**: `sync-btn` relabelled **Refresh** (behaviour unchanged, title/confirm reworded to say what it keeps and to point at Reset), and a new `reset-btn` → `resetClientState()`. Reset clears, in order: every `erp_swr_*` entry in sessionStorage (the stale-while-revalidate cache behind every list/metadata read — collected into an array *before* deleting, since removing while iterating by index reindexes and silently skips every other key), `erp_nav_state`, the in-memory `state` caches, the POS till via the new silent `clearPOSTillState()` (split out of `resetPOSTerminal` so the two resets cannot drift), then re-fetches labels/doctypes/permissions and re-renders.
- [x] **53.17a Found while building 53.17, and the reason Reset would otherwise have been a lie: the lazy view modules had no cache story at all.** `loadViewModule` did `import('view-pos.js')` with **no version parameter** — an ordinary HTTP GET, so (a) after a deploy a browser keeps running the previous release's screen code while the shell updates, and (b) Reset would have cleared every app-level cache and then re-imported the *same stale module* from the HTTP cache, which is exactly the "refresh keeps the cache" complaint the item exists to fix. Fixed with two additions to the URL, both reusing the existing param-composition rather than a second mechanism: a `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` constant (**must be kept in step with `index.html`'s `app.js?v=N` on every release** — documented in ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.0.6's deploy note) and a `reset=<timestamp>` bust set by Reset, which also re-runs each module's top-level state (frozen till, locked store) back to initial values. BLD-041's existing `retry=` behaviour is untouched. All three URL forms verified serving `200 text/javascript` against a scratch server.
- [x] **53.17b Three deliberate non-actions, each a data-safety call rather than an omission.** (1) **Reset refuses to run at all while offline POS sales are queued**, with a message naming the count — `erp_pos_offline_queue` holds *completed sales that have not reached the server*, the customer has walked out with the goods, and that is not cache. Same posture `closePOSSessionFlow` already takes for the same reason. The guard runs *before* the confirm dialog, since there is no point confirming an action that must not proceed. (2) **It does not sign the user out** — resetting a screen must not eject a cashier mid-shift. (3) **It does not reset the theme** — a personal preference is not stale data. Every storage read/write is wrapped in try/catch (private browsing, quota, disabled storage): a cache that cannot be read cannot be stale either, so none of those is a failure worth stopping for.
- [x] **53.17c Docs.** `USER_GUIDE.md` new **§12.3 "When a screen shows something that can't be right — Refresh vs Reset"** — a two-row comparison table, an explicit *"reloading the page in your browser is not the same as Reset"* (F5 re-runs against the same stored copies, so the stale data comes straight back — the trap that makes a stale screen seem incurable), the four things Reset never touches, and a pointer that at the till you usually want **New Sale (Reset)** or **Reset Terminal** instead. Plus a new "How do I…?" row. `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` new **§B.3.0.6** with the exact clear-order including the lazy-module half, the three non-actions and the `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` deploy note. `app.js?v=` bumped 29 → 30 (and `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` set to match). Verified live on a scratch server: both buttons render with the right labels, the served `app.js` carries `resetClientState` and the offline guard, `view-pos.js` carries `clearPOSTillState`, and the versioned/busted/retry module URLs all serve. Shell accessibility/layout invariant tests green.
- [x] **53.18 The 53.1 backfill only ever covered `tenant_default` — found while preparing the 53.15 deploy (2026-10-06).** `migrations_stage53_1_location_sellable.sql` declares the field and backfills it in `tenant_default` only, but `ValidatePOSSellableLocation` (53.2) ships in the **binary**, so it guards every provisioned tenant the moment the new build starts. On any other schema that left no `sellable` doctype_field (so an admin could not even see or correct the flag) and no value on any `Location` row, which falls through `LocationIsSellable` to its `type = 'Store'` test — and 17.9 seeded every in-use location with a hardcoded `type = 'Warehouse'`. That is 53.1's own all-Warehouse outage, guarded for one schema out of N: **every till in every non-default tenant refusing every sale**, which for prod means the `minn` tenant. New `db/migrations_stage53_18_location_sellable_all_tenants.sql` runs both halves of 53.1 across every `tenant\_%` schema, looping in the style of `migrations_stage30_2_1_grn_location.sql`; statements are dollar-quoted (`$sql$`) rather than single-quoted because the quadrupled-quote form a single-quoted `format()` needs is unreadable and was in fact written wrong the first time. `tenant_default` is included rather than excluded — both statements are idempotent, so re-covering it changes nothing and keeps the file correct standalone. **Verified on a disposable DB** reproducing the trap across two tenant schemas: a Warehouse-typed location with a `POSCart` → `Yes`, one with a `POSSession` → `Yes`, a never-sold Warehouse → `No`, a typed Store → `Yes`, an already-`Yes` row untouched, a `public_decoy` schema untouched (the `LIKE 'tenant\_%' ESCAPE` holds), and byte-identical state after two further runs. **Then on the dev DB**: `HO`/`WH01` preserved as the only two sellable, and **9 blank Locations created after 53.1 ran** (all `*-TEST` fixtures, all Warehouse, none with POS history) correctly resolved to `No` — a gap 53.1 had left open on `tenant_default` itself, since 53.1b's save-time stamp only fires on write and never touches a row nobody edits. 110 `No` + 2 `Yes` = 112. `go build`/`go vet`/`./db` tests clean. **Does not remove 53.15's review requirement**: this guarantees no location that has ever sold stops selling, but a genuinely trading shop with no POS history in the database still resolves to `No`, which remains a business answer only the user can confirm.
- [ ] **53.15 Deploy.** Holds behind the same batched-deploy decision as Stage 51 — do not ship to the `minn` production tenant until told to. **The 53.1 migration needs a production backfill review first**, and 53.1's dev-DB result is the reason: which locations genuinely sell is a business answer, and the evidence-based rule will still mark a newly-opened shop with no POS history as not-sellable. Walk Setup → Core → Location on prod after migrating and confirm every trading shop reads `Sellable = Yes` (documented for the admin in ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.0.4).
  - *2026-10-06: the `tenant_default`-only scope of the 53.1 backfill was found and fixed first — see 53.18. Deployed on the user's instruction ("deploy as well once all pushed"), which lifts the "until told to" hold. The Setup → Core → Location walk below is still outstanding and is the user's to do: this session cannot read production (classifier-blocked), so no one has yet confirmed that every trading `minn` shop reads `Sellable = Yes`.*
- [ ] **53.16 Found live, not caused by this Stage, needs a product decision: the initial-JS release budget is now over.** `cmd/releasebudget`'s `initial_js_gzip_bytes` gate (NFR-COST-001, ≤120 KiB gzip) fails at **129,143 bytes — over by 6,263**. Measured contribution: this Stage's two large `view-pos.js` blocks gzip to **8,862 bytes**, so Stage 53 is what crossed the line. But the structural issue is older and not this Stage's to decide: `jsFiles` in `cmd/releasebudget/main.go:36` still counts **`public/view-pos.js`**, which **BLD-041 made lazy-loaded** (`import()` only after the route entitlement check) — it is not startup JavaScript any more, yet it sits in a budget whose own note reads *"startup JavaScript compressed individually"*. That list looks stale against the in-flight app.js→view-*.js split (which is another session's uncommitted work — `public/view-pos.js` is not in HEAD at all, and `public/app.js` is 1.14 MB at HEAD vs 309 KB on disk). The test message says *"shrink the artifact rather than raising the limit"*, so the budget list was **deliberately not edited here**. **[needs design decision: user — either (a) drop the lazy-loaded view modules from `jsFiles` so the gate measures genuine startup JS, which is what it says it measures, or (b) keep them in and shrink `view-pos.js`]**. Two other suite failures confirmed **not** from this Stage: `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` (drifts on `GET /api/v1/ready`, a route from another session, plus the migration count) and `TestDriftGuardsUnmappedScreensSummary` (expects a `lazy-module-view` screen mapping, BLD-041's work). `TestDrillIncidentTwoStaleBackup` fails only in a full-suite run and passes in isolation — the shared-DB ordering flake this repo already knows about.


## Stage 54 — Version identity in the UI (opened + built 2026-10-04 from the user's request)

User asked for "a version system in erp. Std one. It will show near profile. Each deploy should update automatically and user friendly one and release/build date as well." Scoped on inspection: the **backend already existed** (Stage 14.6) — `internal/server/VERSION` is `go:embed`ed, `gitCommit`/`buildTime` are `-ldflags`-stamped by `manage.ps1`, `deploy/deploy.ps1`, `promote.ps1` and CI, and `GET /api/v1/version` has been public since 14.6. Nothing surfaced any of it in the app. So this Stage is the UI plus the one derived field it needed, not a new version system.

Three product calls came from the user mid-build: **keep the version at `0.1.0`** (bumping it would desync ~47 governance docs carrying `applies_to: source release 0.1.0`, and nothing about the UI requires a different number); **version and date only** — the git commit and build timestamp were explicitly rejected as "not user friendly", so an About dialog, a copy-for-support action and the commit/build-time rows that were built first were all removed again before this landed; and after seeing it live, **the date came off the screen entirely** (see 54.3a).

- [x] **54.1** `releaseDate()` (`internal/server/middleware.go`), beside the existing `currentAppVersion()`/`gitCommit`/`buildTime` block. Parses the ldflags-injected `buildTime` and returns the calendar day as `YYYY-MM-DD` — **derived, not stored separately**, so the release date can never drift from the build it names. Returns `""` for the `"unknown"` default a bare `go build` leaves and for anything unparseable, which is the signal the frontend keys off to render a development build rather than a wrong or zero-value date.
- [x] **54.2** `GET /api/v1/version` gained `release_date`. Kept a **flat `map[string]string`** deliberately: `TestVersionEndpointIsPublicAndTenantStampingWorks` and any existing ops caller decode this response into a string map, so a non-string field here would be a silent breaking change. A `product` field was added and then removed again in the same pass once the About dialog was dropped — nothing consumed it.
- [x] **54.3** The sidebar line itself. `public/index.html` gains one `<p class="app-version-line" id="app-version-line">` inside the existing `.sidebar-footer`, directly under the account chip, plus one `<p id="account-popover-version">` as the last line of the existing account popover; `public/styles.css` gains `.app-version-line` and `.account-popover-version`, appended to the existing token vocabulary — no new component, no new dialog system. `loadAppVersion()` in `public/app.js` fills both, with `formatReleaseDate()` rendering the ISO date in the **viewer's own locale** via `toLocaleDateString` (so "Oct 4, 2026" or "4 Oct 2026" depending on the browser, rather than a hardcoded format). Deliberately plain `<p>`s, not controls: this is reference information a user glances at when reporting a problem, never something to click.
- [x] **54.3a** Format revised after the user saw it live (superseded again by 54.3c - kept because the contrast reasoning below still governs). The first cut read `v0.1.0 · Oct 4, 2026`; the user rejected it as not standard enough and asked for smaller. **Second cut (superseded by 54.3c): the sidebar showed `Version 0.1.0` alone** — the spelled-out enterprise form (SAP/Dynamics/Tally), and the shortest useful string — with the release date moved to that line's `title` tooltip *and* to the last line of the account popover (`Version 0.1.0 · Released 4 Oct 2026`). The popover copy exists because a `title` tooltip never appears on a touch device and nobody discovers one by accident; "click your own name" is the discoverable path to the date, and it sits next to Sign Out where support-ticket information belongs. Size dropped 11px → 10px, letter-spacing 0.01em → 0.02em. **The colour was deliberately NOT lightened** even though the user chose "smaller and lighter": `--text-muted` (`#64748b`) on white measures 4.76:1, already close to the 4.5:1 WCAG AA floor that applies to body text at any size, so one step lighter would have failed. The extra quietness is bought with size instead — recorded here so nobody "finishes the job" by lightening it later.
- [x] **54.3b** Pre-existing dark-mode defect found while contrast-checking 54.3a, fixed in the same pass with the user's approval: `.account-trigger` is a `<button>` and set no `color`, so the username inside inherited the **UA default button colour — pure black**. Fine on white (17.85:1), unreadable on the dark sidebar at **1.35:1**. `.user-role` escaped it only because it sets its own colour, which is exactly why the role line looked right and the name did not. One declaration (`color: var(--text-main)`) on `.account-trigger` fixes it at the single place every element in the chip inherits from, rather than per-child. Measured 1.35:1 → **12.99:1** dark, 17.85:1 retained light.
- [x] **54.3c** Third and final format revision, again from the user looking at it live. `Version 0.1.0` in the sidebar with a native `title` tooltip reading `Version 0.1.0 / Released Oct 4, 2026` was rejected: *"No. Only this info and very small pop up. Clean compact small UI. 0.1.0 / 4/10/26"*. Every label is gone. **The sidebar shows `0.1.0` alone**; hover, keyboard focus or tap opens a **custom popup** — two lines, `0.1.0` over `4/10/26`, no heading, no labels, no actions — replacing the native `title`, which cannot be styled, cannot be made small, and never appears on a touch device at all. The popup opens upward (`bottom: 100%`) because this sits at the very bottom of the sidebar, and is positioned relative to a new `.app-version` wrapper so it travels with the line instead of needing measured coordinates. The line became a real `<button>` (styled back down to plain text) now that it opens something: that is what makes it keyboard-reachable and tappable. Closes on leave, blur, Escape or an outside click. The `account-popover-version` line added in 54.3a was **removed** — the popup is reachable by tap, so it was the redundancy that convention was working around.
- [x] **54.3d** `formatReleaseDate()` rewritten from `toLocaleDateString` to parsing the ISO parts directly and emitting `D/M/YY`. Locale formatting was wrong here twice over: on this machine's US-locale browser it renders month-first (`10/4/26`), **inverting day and month for an Indian reader**, and it cannot be pinned to day-first without hardcoding a locale anyway — at which point it is no longer locale-aware and the stdlib call buys nothing. Parsing `^(\d{4})-(\d{2})-(\d{2})$` and formatting by hand is deterministic, matches the format the user asked for exactly, and still returns `''` for the unstamped-build case (rendered as `dev build` in the popup).
- [x] **54.3e** Typeface: the first cut of the popup used `--font-numeric` (monospace) on the theory that version strings want tabular digits. Live it read wide and technical against the sidebar's UI font two lines above. Both the line and the popup now use `--font-family`, which blends into the shell — the "clean" half of the user's ask. Judgement call made from the screenshot, not asked for.
- [x] **54.3f** Placement (user QA, 2026-10-17: *"Versioning took space in bottom adjust in the profile itself. Remove a extra row. Bring right corner of profile and not add bottom row or another column to erp menu. Adjust in the same width."*). The `.app-version` wrapper moved from its own row under the chip into `.account-menu`, absolutely positioned at the chip's **bottom-right corner** (`right: 8px; bottom: 10px`) — on the role line, directly under the chevron, which now aligns to the name line (`align-self: flex-start; margin-top: 4px`) instead of the chip's centre. No row, no column: the chip keeps its 52px height and the sidebar its width. It stays a sibling of the trigger, not a child, because a `<button>` cannot nest in a `<button>`. The role line gained ellipsis + `padding-right: 14px` so a long role truncates before reaching a wider version like `0.12.10`; the popup now anchors `right: 0`, opening upward from the sidebar's right edge. `styles.css?v=28→29`. Verified in Chrome via Playwright against the real stylesheet: chevron centred on the name line, version centred on the role line, both 8px in from the chip edge, no overlap with a long role, popup inside the sidebar.
- [x] **54.4** Fetched with a **plain `fetch`, not `apiFetch`** (the route is public, so the line must still render when a tenant/permission call fails) and with `cache: 'no-store'` — the entire point is to reflect the binary running right now, not what a cached response said before a deploy. Both elements ship `hidden` and are revealed only once the fetch resolves, so a failed/offline fetch leaves no empty row or orphaned separator behind.
- [x] **54.5** Cache-busting, so a deploy actually reaches a returning user: `app.js?v=27→31` and `styles.css?v=22→26` across the four passes. `staticAssetCache` (`internal/server/middleware_compress.go`) already serves `index.html` as `no-cache` and any `?v=`-stamped asset as `immutable` for a year, so without the bump an existing session would have kept the old JS/CSS and never shown the line.
- [x] **54.6** `Dockerfile` now stamps build identity too (`ARG GIT_COMMIT=dev` / `ARG BUILD_TIME=unknown` feeding the same `-X custom_erp/internal/server.gitCommit/buildTime` ldflags every other build path uses), so a container build reports the same version/release date as a native one instead of permanently reading "development build". Both ARGs default to the unstamped values, so a bare `docker build` is unchanged. The container path is still dormant (see the Dockerfile's own header); this just stops it being wrong the day it is used. The `releasebudget` build at `ci.yml:84` was deliberately **left unstamped** — it measures artifact size and is never shipped.
- [x] **54.7** Tests: new `internal/server/version_release_test.go` — `TestReleaseDateDerivesFromBuildStamp` (stamped build, the `"unknown"` default, empty, and unparseable) and `TestVersionPayloadShape`, which pins the flat-string-map contract and all four fields. Both are **DB-free** on purpose, unlike the existing 14.6 test, so they still run when no Postgres is up. `go vet ./internal/server/` and `node --check public/app.js` clean. **Live-verified on every format revision**: a binary built with the real deploy ldflags returned `{"build_time":"2026-10-04T08:49:51Z","git_commit":"afaf419","release_date":"2026-10-04","version":"0.1.0"}`, and the final Playwright pass as `manager1` confirmed the sidebar line reads `0.1.0` at 10px, the popup reads `0.1.0` over `4/10/26`, and that it opens on hover, on keyboard focus and on click while closing on pointer-leave, Escape and outside click — in both themes, zero page errors. Contrast measures 4.76:1 light / 6.07:1 dark. The one console 403 seen during those passes (`/api/v1/admin/industry/lock`) is pre-existing and role-related, not from this change.
- [x] **54.8** Guide docs. `USER_GUIDE.md` §11.1 ("Which version you are on") plus a "How do I…?" row, written against the final design — where the line is, the two ways to reach the date, what `Development build` means, and why to quote it in a bug report. `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` §C.2.1 covers the operational half: the full `/api/v1/version` payload, which half is hand-maintained (`internal/server/VERSION`) versus automatic (the ldflags build stamp), the three-step bump procedure, why the version is never set anywhere but that file, what `development build` on a *server* means (someone built outside the normal scripts — do not trust that deploy), and the ~47 docs worth sweeping alongside a version bump.

**Note for whoever bumps the release number**: `internal/server/VERSION` is the single source. Nothing else needs touching for the UI — the date follows the build automatically. ~47 docs under `docs/` carry `applies_to: source release 0.1.0` in their frontmatter and would want a sweep at the same time; `cmd/doclint` only checks that the key exists, not its value, so they will not fail the build if left behind.

> **Lost once to a concurrent session.** This section was appended, then silently clobbered when a parallel session wrote its own Stage 53 updates over `micro_checklist.md`; it was detected only because a follow-up edit to 54.3 found nothing to match. Re-appended 2026-10-04. If Stage 54 is missing again, this is why — check `git diff` on this file before assuming it was never written.

  errors; the four earlier `onPOSScanKeyDown` errors did not reproduce. The paced 53-view audit is

## 2026-10-09 five-item pass (suite green, 56.9, 49.4.4, Purchase Return GST reversal, release)

- [x] **1 — Full suite green; `TestAuditDeletedRowIsDetectedByCheckpoint` root cause.** Both
  dev-DB debris **and** a real defect. The debris was checkpoint `f92a1358…` (seq 1..491731) in
  `custom_erp.tenant_default`, written 2026-10-04 by server process `pid-3324`. That process
  ran with a one-off `JWT_SECRET`. Its own verify job (`async_jobs`) reported `checkpoints_ok:1,
  verified:0`, while every later process (`pid-11144`, `pid-23652`, the tests) shows the
  reverse, and the local key verifies both later checkpoints but not this one under any tenant
  id. The defect behind it: audit rows, checkpoints and archive manifests were HMAC'd with
  `jwtSigningKey`, the session key Stage 29.8 rotates, so any JWT rotation turned all earlier
  evidence into "tampered". Fix (`engines/audit_evidence.go`, `audit_archive.go`): sig version
  `v2-hmac-sha256` signs with a dedicated `AUDIT_SIGNING_KEY` keyring (`loadVersionedKeyring`,
  `_<n>` rotation); `v1` evidence still verifies against every JWT keyring key; unknown
  versions stay out of coverage. New `TestAuditEvidenceSurvivesJWTKeyRotation`; baseline
  finding **SB-025** (warn) when the key is unset; `deploy/erp.env.example` documents it. The
  debris row was backed up (scratchpad JSON) and deleted from the dev DB **with the user's
  OK**. Proof: full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` exits 0 in a clean HEAD worktree plus this
  fix. In the live tree, `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` also drifts (migrations 175→176).
  That drift is erp-d1's untracked `migrations_stage59_1_bin_allocation.sql`, and the test
  passes at clean HEAD (175 files). It is regenerated at release.

- [x] **3 — 49.4.4 shared request envelope** (see Stage 49's 49.4.4 entry; follow-ups in 49.4.4a).
- [x] **4 — GST input-credit reversal on Purchase Returns** (follow-up to 57.15; model approved by
  the user 2026-10-10).
  - **Where:** `PostPurchaseReturn`, in the same transaction as the stock-out, through new
    `purchaseReturnITCReversal` (`engines/purchase_return.go`). It posts Dr 2100 / Cr 1500
    (idempotency key `PurchaseReturn:<id>:ITC_REVERSAL`) only when the GRN's standing invoices
    (Matched/Approved/Pending Approval/Paid) exceed what it is owed after this return.
  - **Amount:** the excess, capped at the returned goods' tax-inclusive value, times the bills' own
    GST share. Nothing is reversed for set-aside stock or for a foreign-currency GRN.
  - **Shared basis:** the GRN billing computation was extracted from `MatchVendorInvoice` into
    `readGRNBilling` (`engines/vendor_invoice.go`), so the match and the reversal value goods the
    same way. The existing match tests are unchanged and pass.
  - **Debit note:** records `gst_amount` and `total_with_gst`, and its Reason ends with "GST X
    reversed; total with GST Y" because the notes list shows only Amount and Reason. The UI toast
    now reports the reversal (`view-procurement.js`, its own hunk; asset version 35 → 36).
  - **GST Return Summary:** unchanged code (`reports.go` belongs to the Stage 55 session). Its ITC
    is 1500's period net, so it drops by the reversal.
  - **Tests:** `TestPurchaseReturnReversesClaimedInputGST` covers three cases. Paid before return
    reverses 54.00, leaves 1500 at 126.00 and 2100 at Dr 354.00, writes the note fields and Reason,
    and the summary ITC falls by 54.00. Returned before billing posts no 1500 entry, and the later
    7-unit bill ends at 126.00. Billed but unpaid reverses 54.00 at return and nets to 126.00 after
    payment.
  - **Live on scratch :8147:** Chromium posted a real return against a paid bill. The response
    said `gst_reversed: 54`, the toast showed the reversal, and the Debit Notes list showed the
    Reason suffix. GL rows matched the model exactly. The fixture was removed afterwards.
  - **Docs:** ADMIN_GUIDE Purchase Returns items 6 and 8 ("reverse it by hand" replaced) and
    USER_GUIDE §6.5.

## Stage 56 — Railway Pro migration (opened 2026-10-04 from the user's decision)

The user purchased a **Railway annual plan (₹3,000/year)** and decided to proceed; the DigitalOcean
droplet is ₹500/month. Assessment on opening found the app is already largely PaaS-ready and the
work is smaller than a provider migration usually is: migrations are `go:embed`ed into the binary
(`db/migrate.go:19`) so Railway's pre-deploy command can apply them with no `db/` folder on the
host; an unset `HOST` binds every interface (`internal/server/routes.go:1190`), which is what
Railway needs; every secret already has an env override (`JWT_SECRET`, `CHANNEL_CREDENTIAL_KEY`,
`AUDIT_ARCHIVE_KEY` — the config-dir file fallbacks in `engines/auth.go:61` /
`engines/secret_keyring.go:123` are dev-only paths production already bypasses); job claiming uses
`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`engines/jobrunner.go:261`), so Railway's overlapping rolling deploys
cannot double-run a job; and the `Dockerfile` — dormant since 2026-07-27, built by CI's
`docker-build-check` on every push so it cannot rot — already produces a distroless static image
stamped with build identity (54.6).

**Architecture decision, recorded because the alternative was the user's first instinct:** this is
**one** Railway app + **one** Railway Postgres serving **many** tenant schemas, via
`TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` (`internal/server/tenant_host.go`, Stage 44) and `tenantctl provision`.
It is **not** one Railway app per client. One-app-per-client multiplies the metered bill, the
deploys, the migration runs, the backup checks and the version-drift risk by the client count, and
bypasses the multi-tenancy engine already built. Hosting many clients is the goal; separate
instances are not the mechanism. A contractual physical-isolation requirement from a single large
client is the one legitimate exception, and is a priced premium tier, not the default.

**Production stays on the droplet for all of 56.1-56.10.** Nothing in those items touches the live
box, so there is no window where a client is at risk. 56.7/56.8 exist because the two things that
actually decide this migration — metered cost and India latency — are measurements, not
assumptions, and both are now cheap to take since the plan is already paid for.

- [ ] **56.1 Boot on Railway with the security baseline in warn mode.** New Railway project, app
  service from this repo's `Dockerfile`, Railway Postgres service alongside. Leave `ENV` **unset**
  so `engines/security_baseline.go` logs every `[SECURITY] SB-nnn` finding without blocking the
  boot — the point of this step is to read the full finding list off a real deploy rather than
  predict it. Set `JWT_SECRET`, `CHANNEL_CREDENTIAL_KEY`, `AUDIT_ARCHIVE_KEY` from
  `openssl rand`, leave `HOST` unset, let Railway supply `PORT`. Pre-deploy command
  `./erp-server -migrate`; confirm against `-migrate-status` that the count matches the 169
  migrations `custom_erp_t1` carries locally. Done when `/api/v1/version` answers over Railway's
  generated domain and reports this commit.
- [ ] **56.2 SB-015 — encrypted database link.** `engines/security_baseline.go:283` blocks a
  production boot when `DATABASE_URL` names a non-loopback host with an `sslmode` that permits
  cleartext. On the droplet Postgres is on loopback so the rule sleeps; on Railway the database is
  a separate service, so it fires correctly. Add `sslmode=require` and confirm the negotiated
  connection with `SELECT ssl FROM pg_stat_ssl WHERE pid = pg_backend_pid();` — the setting being
  present is not evidence it was honoured. Prefer `verify-full` + `sslrootcert=` if Railway
  publishes a CA for its Postgres; record which mode was actually achieved and why.
- [ ] **56.3 SB-012 — proxy trust against Railway's edge.** `engines/security_baseline.go:251`
  blocks production when `TRUST_PROXY` is on and `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` is empty, because
  `clientIP()` (`internal/server/middleware.go:606`) would then accept any caller's own
  `X-Forwarded-For` — forging the identity that login rate limiting, lockout and the audit trail
  all record. Railway does not publish its edge proxy's address range, so this is **determined
  empirically, not from the code**: with 56.1 still in warn mode, log `r.RemoteAddr` on one real
  inbound request, then pin that range (expect a private IPv6 or CGNAT block) into
  `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS`. Verify by sending a request with a forged `X-Forwarded-For` and
  confirming the resolved IP is the socket peer, not the header. Record the range and the date —
  if Railway renumbers, this silently degrades to trusting a header, so it needs a re-check item
  at cutover and a note in the runbook.
- [ ] **56.4 Volume for `media_store`.** `engines/pim_media.go:39` hardcodes `mediaStoreDir =
  "media_store"` as a relative path and the image's `WORKDIR` is `/app`, so a Railway volume
  mounted at `/app/media_store` lines up with no code change. `engines/pim_dam.go:138` writes its
  render cache to the same directory. Attach the volume, upload a product image **and a
  thumbnail-generating one**, redeploy, and confirm both survive — a container's ephemeral disk is
  wiped per deploy, so an unmounted path loses every uploaded photo on the next release and does so
  silently. Note for 56.12: a service with a volume cannot run multiple replicas on Railway, which
  caps this at vertical scaling — the same ceiling the droplet has, so no regression, but it
  removes horizontal autoscaling as a reason to be here at all.
- [ ] **56.5 Flip `ENV=production` and confirm a clean boot.** With 56.2/56.3 fixed, production
  mode must start with no blocking finding. Also set `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` to the Railway domain
  (SB-008/011 block otherwise) and confirm SB-021 does not fire — `ENV=production` refuses to boot
  while the seed admin credential is still active (`internal/server/routes.go:40`), so a real admin
  with MFA must exist on this instance first, exactly as `deploy/README.md` Part D sequences it.
- [ ] **56.6 Prove multi-tenant hosting end to end — this is the item that makes the money.** Set
  `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN`, point a wildcard DNS record at the Railway app, then
  `tenantctl provision` two test clients and confirm `client1.<domain>` and `client2.<domain>` each
  resolve to their own schema with no data bleed between them, both served by the single app on the
  single meter. Check `db/db.go`'s shared `dbMaxOpenConns = 50` headroom while both are live —
  go_live_decisions.md §4a.1 already flags the connection pool as a nearer ceiling than tenant
  count, and it is one pool for every tenant.
- [ ] **56.7 Measure the meter for 5 days.** The open economic question. Railway bills metered
  compute/RAM/volume/egress **on top of** the plan fee, and the plan fee is per-seat, not a hosting
  cap — so "₹3,000/year vs ₹500/month" is not yet a like-for-like comparison and must not be
  treated as one. Run 56.1-56.6 live for five days with real clicking, then read Railway's own
  usage dashboard for actual ₹/month at this workload. Costs nothing extra (the year is paid) and
  replaces an estimate with a fact. Record the number here. **Plan confirmed 2026-10-07: Railway Pro** — per-seat fee plus metered usage, so the which-plan question in this item is closed and the metered half is the only number still open.
- [ ] **56.8 Measure India latency.** Railway has no India region; nearest is Singapore, against
  the droplet's Bangalore. go_live_decisions.md:180 weighted India latency deliberately for
  GST/e-invoice/Pine Labs. Measure p50/p95 on a POS add-line and a receipt print from an Indian
  connection, both providers, same build. A cashier scanning items feels this; an admin editing a
  master does not. Verify Railway's current region list first — it changes.
- [x] **56.9 Rework backup monitoring from folder-stat to a database record.** *Built and verified
  2026-10-09 (five-item pass, item 2).*
  - **Table:** new `db/migrations_stage56_9_backup_runs.sql` creates `public.backup_runs` in the
    public schema only, since a backup is a whole-database fact. It is additive
    (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` + `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) and applied twice locally to prove
    it is idempotent.
  - **backup.sh:** `deploy/backup.sh`'s `record_run` writes one row per run (scope
    `database`/`tenant`, outcome, file, size, duration, sha256, host). A failed run writes
    `Failed` with its exit detail. A row that cannot be written keeps the backup and posts
    `BACKUP_RECORD_FAILED`.
  - **Monitor:** `CheckBackupFreshness` reads the newest successful `database` row, so tenant
    exports never count (26.1.6). `StartBackupFreshnessMonitor` always runs. Under
    `ENV=production`, no row is an hourly alert plus boot finding **SB-026** (warn, so a fresh
    install can still deploy). Off production it stays quiet until a run is recorded.
  - **Tests:** `TestBackupFreshness` and `TestBackupStaleAlertReachesTheWebhook` cover fresh,
    stale + latest-failed, missing in dev and production, tenant export and failed run. The
    BLD-052 drill now injects a stale row.
  - **Drill:** a real `backup.sh` run on scratch DBs covered whole-DB success, tenant success,
    tenant failure and a DB without the table. Each row's sha256 matched its file.
  - **Shipping:** `deploy.ps1` now re-ships `deploy/backup.sh` (+ `chmod 755`) every deploy,
    as it already did `remote_deploy.sh`.
  - **Docs:** ADMIN_GUIDE §D, `backup_restore.md` and `incident_runbook.md` are updated.

  Original item text: The real code work
  in this Stage, and an improvement independent of hosting. `engines/alerting.go:297` resolves
  `BACKUP_DIR` (default `/opt/erp/backups`) and `CheckBackupFreshness` stats the newest
  `custom_erp_*.dump.enc` in it — and at `engines/alerting.go:310` treats a **missing directory as
  "not a machine that takes backups" and returns silently**. Correct on a dev laptop; on a PaaS it
  means backup monitoring switches itself off and reports nothing, which is the worst available
  failure mode. It also cannot be fixed by pointing `BACKUP_DIR` at a volume, because Railway does
  not share a volume between services: the app checking the folder and the job writing to it are
  different services and cannot see each other's disk. Fix: additive `backup_runs` table (new
  incremental migration), the backup job writes a row on success with size/duration/outcome, and
  `CheckBackupFreshness` reads the latest row instead of stat-ing a directory. Keep the
  `tenant_*`-exports-do-not-count rule (26.1.6). Separately, make "no backup mechanism configured"
  a **loud** finding when `ENV=production` rather than silence, on both providers.
- [ ] **56.10 Stand up the backup job itself on Railway.** A cron-scheduled service running the
  `pg_dump` → encrypt (`BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`) → retain path that `deploy/backup.sh` does today,
  writing its own volume plus the 56.9 row, with Railway's native Postgres backups enabled as a
  second independent copy rather than the only one. Then a **real restore drill** into a scratch
  database per `docs/operations/backup_restore.md` — a schedule existing is not evidence a backup
  restores, which is the lesson 2026-08-07 already taught on the droplet. Must pass before 56.11.
- [ ] **56.11 Decision gate — migrate production, or keep Railway as staging/demo.**
  `[needs design decision: the user's call, on 56.7 + 56.8 numbers, not on the plan fee]`. Inputs:
  measured ₹/month, measured India latency, and what is being given up —
  `deploy/remote_deploy.sh`'s staged `.prev` snapshots with commit-matched health gating and
  automatic rollback (Stage 50/BLD-005/BLD-006), `deploy/postgres_harden.sql`'s least-privilege
  `erp_app`/`erp_migrate`/`erp_backup` split, and the 49.7.3 `ufw`/port-boundary verification, none
  of which port to a PaaS as-is. If the numbers favour the droplet, Railway still earns the year as
  a **demo/trial instance** — sandbox provisioning already exists (`handleProvisionSandboxTenant`,
  Stage 38.7) and a demo that idles keeps the meter near zero. Either outcome uses what was bought;
  only one moves client data.
- [ ] **56.12 Cutover — only if 56.11 says go.** Real domain + TLS on Railway,
  `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` repointed, database moved (small: §26.10.6 measured production as holding
  no transactional data, so this is minutes — re-measure before trusting it), re-run the 56.3
  proxy-range check, full module smoke pass, then DNS cutover with **the droplet kept warm for a
  week** as the rollback. Decommission only after that week. Per the standing release principle: no
  client data impacted, no already-set password changed.
- [ ] **56.13 Docs.** `docs/operations/go_live_decisions.md` §4 gains the revisit entry and the
  outcome (the §4 "decided 2026-08-06 — the droplet is production" call is what this Stage
  reopens); `deploy/README.md` gains or yields to a Railway runbook; `docs/ai_handover.md` §6
  records the new deploy path and dev-server state; `USER_GUIDE`/`ADMIN_GUIDE` need a pass if and
  when 56.6's per-client subdomains become customer-facing, which they do.

**2026-10-07 — pre-emptive fix landed for 56.1's expected first failure (`Dockerfile`, uncommitted).**
At the user's request, rather than waiting for the deploy to fail: the runtime stage's base image is
now `ARG RUNTIME_BASE=gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12:debug`, used as `FROM ${RUNTIME_BASE}`. The
`:debug` variant is the same distroless image plus a busybox `/bin/sh` and nothing else — same
`nonroot` user, same static-only contents. Reason: a PaaS pre-deploy/release hook runs its command
*through a shell*, so `./erp-server -migrate` on the shell-free image fails with
`exec: "sh": executable file not found`, which reads like a missing binary and is not one. Made an
overridable ARG rather than a hardcoded swap so the shell-free image can be pinned back with
`--build-arg RUNTIME_BASE=gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12` without editing the file.
**The ARG is declared global (before the first `FROM`) deliberately** — declared inside the builder
stage it is out of scope at the runtime `FROM`, expands to empty, and the build dies on a bare
`FROM`; that mistake was made and corrected while writing this.
Two caveats, both load-bearing:
- **Unverified by build.** Docker is not installed on this machine (`docker: command not found`), so
  this has never been built. CI's `docker-build-check` is the first thing that will exercise it, and
  Railway the first thing that will boot it. Do not record it as working until one of those passes.
- **It cannot reach Railway until it is pushed.** Railway builds from GitHub, which is at `c90e5ca`
  (2026-10-06); this change is uncommitted in the working tree. Until a commit of `Dockerfile`
  reaches `origin/main`, 56.1 will still hit the original shell failure. `Dockerfile`'s only
  uncommitted hunk is this change (54.6's build-identity ARGs are already committed in `c90e5ca`),
  so it is cleanly committable on its own — but committing/pushing is the user's call, not a routine
  dev step, per the Git Safety Protocol.
- **Retire this before 56.12** if a container path ever becomes production: a shell in the runtime
  image widens the Stage 49.S2 attack surface and is justified only while the migrate hook needs it.
  Either run migrations as a one-off job that bypasses a shell, or confirm the platform can invoke
  the hook in exec form. Today's droplet production runs the plain binary under systemd and is
  unaffected by this line.

**Carried from the opening assessment, so it is not rediscovered:** the four items that read as
alarming (56.2, 56.3, 56.4, 56.9) are ~1 to 1.5 days of work in total, not the bulk of this Stage.
Three are config-sized once the empirical value in 56.3 is known. The weight is in 56.9/56.10
(backups, done properly) and 56.12 (verification and a safe cutover) — the same shape as any
provider move: the container was never the hard part.

## Stage 57 — User QA round: cross-cutting UI invariants and new scope (opened 2026-10-04 from the user's live report)

Full triage, with verbatim reports, root causes and answers: [user QA round 2026-10-04](product/erp-user-qa-2026-10-04.md).
The user operated the **deployed** build and actually submitted transactions (PO, GRN, TO, Bin,
Vendor) — the real-user acceptance Stage 55.0 explicitly disclaims. Confirmed defects from that
round are filed against the matching open Stage 55 sub-items rather than duplicated here; this
Stage holds only the **cross-cutting invariants** and the **new scope** Stage 55 does not authorize.

**Already fixed this pass:** the bulk-import template download asked for browser credentials
because `GET /api/v1/import/{doctype}/template` is behind `apiMiddleware` while
`downloadImportTemplate` used a plain anchor `href` (no `Authorization` header, so a 401 and the
browser's own prompt). It now fetches via `apiFetch` and saves a blob — `public/app.js`.

### Cross-cutting invariants (one choke point each, not a per-screen sweep)

- [x] **57.1 Show the Name, never the document code, in all UI.** The user sees
  `Department/HQ/2026/000001` where a name belongs, and asks for this "everywhere". One
  display-resolution helper for Link fields at the shared renderer; the id stays the submitted
  value, only the label changes. **Highest-leverage item in the round** — it is also the visible
  half of the id-vs-code validator defects (TO `from_warehouse`, credit/debit note PO picker).
  — *2026-10-05/06: built and verified live. `installNameDisplay` (public/app.js) gives every `attachLinkTypeahead` picker a name-showing box whose `.value` still returns the code - no reader changed; a view-wide sweep (`sweepLinkNames`, MutationObserver) swaps names in for `data-link-ref` cells and for cells whose whole text is a master series code `<RecordType>/...` (document numbers like PO/HO/26-27/000002 are left alone; a master's own list keeps its Code column). Browser check: Transfer list shows "Bandra Store" not `Location/HQ/2026/000009`; picker shows the name with value `LOC9`.*
- [x] **57.2 Universal missing-prerequisite pattern.** Today a missing master leaves a half-broken
  form behind the "No Department has been set up yet" prompt. Wanted: a full-screen takeover
  showing only the setup prompt, return-to-origin on browser Back, and the prompt degrading to
  non-blocking once the master exists. Must be one implementation for every prerequisite —
  Department, Zone (Bin creation hard-blocks the same way), HSN, and the rest. Pairs with
  Stage 55.3's "guided dependencies", for which this is the acceptance criterion.
  — *2026-10-05/06: built and verified live. Inline quick-create (public/view-pickers.js, lazy) opens on top of the open form, asks only required fields from /meta with the same server-numbering rules as the full form, seeds the typed text, and fills the field on save. Field setup hints create inline; the screen banner's navigate path now closes the open new-record form, pushes one history entry (browser Back returns), and reopens the form with its values and the new record filled in (`performQuickCreateReturn`). Browser check: PR form -> "Set up Department" -> created "Finance" without the form closing; hint drops to the quiet "Can't find…" line.*
- [x] **57.3 Inline quick-create from the Link picker.** When a typed Item / Vendor / HSN does not
  exist, offer to create it in place, ask only the required fields, and return to the origin form
  with the new record selected. One implementation serving all three (the user raised it
  separately for each). Pairs with Stage 55.3's "lookup/quick-create".
  — *2026-10-05/06: built and verified live for Location (Transfer), Department (PR) and Zone (Bin); every typeahead's "no match" row offers Create "<typed>", generic Link selects end with "+ Create new <Type>…". HSN: new **HSNCode** catalogue doctype (engines/hsn_catalog.go, all tenants, backfilled from existing Items, 4/6/8 digits to match Item's MASTER-0043) feeds a picker on Item.hsn_code; Item still stores the plain number; a saved Item teaches the catalogue (only after the save succeeds - a rejected Item taught it a code in the first cut, caught and fixed).*
- [x] **57.4 `Status*` defaults to Active on every master.** A default on the `Select` field
  definition, applied once across master doctypes, not form by form.
  — *2026-10-05/06: done at the one generic form builder (`defaultSelectValue`) and in quick-create; verified live (New Department -> Status = Active). New records only.*
- [x] **57.5 Rename the docs "On this page" header.** The user wants a standard book/manual
  convention instead. Site-wide label change in the docs shell.
  — *2026-10-05/06: renamed to "Contents" (book/manual convention) and opened by default (public/view-help.js).*
- [x] **57.6 One shared calendar control, used by every calendar in the ERP.** Raised against the
  Appointment Calendar ("make it better/best and smooth") and asked for everywhere. **Constraint:
  no new dependency** — this is built in vanilla JS against the existing `public/` rules.
  — *2026-10-05/06: built and verified live. One vanilla-JS picker (public/view-pickers.js, loaded on first open) replaces the browser's picker on every `input[type=date]` app-wide via one observer; the native input, its YYYY-MM-DD value, typing and min/max are unchanged. Keyboard: Alt+Down/F4, arrows, PageUp/Down, Home/End, Enter, Esc. **Also found and fixed a real bug:** Appointment Calendar's day/week arrows and week columns used `toISOString().slice(0,10)` - in IST that is the previous day, so "next day" did nothing; same bug in two report defaults. Shared `localISODate` fixes all five. Start/End are now time inputs. Browser check: next-day arrow advances, picker navigates and selects.*

### New scope — each needs a decision before any code

- [x] **57.7 Barcode identity at the variant, with configuration.** *Decided by the user 2026-10-06:* **per variant, both toggles** - barcode belongs to the variant/SKU; one setting for when it is generated (SKU creation or GRN) and one for stable-per-SKU vs date-based; Item-level barcode stops being required. Build next.
  Barcode generation is at Item level; the user wants it at variant level, plus two independent
  toggles — generation point (Item-SKU / GRN) and stability (date-derived vs the same barcode for
  a SKU every time), each switchable on. Confirm the model before building: this touches stock
  identity and is not a small change. Check overlap with the Stage 51 GRN-barcode item.
  — *2026-10-06: built and verified.* An Item already is the SKU/variant here (the parent design is a Product Family, which never has a barcode), so: Item.barcode no longer mandatory (all tenants); settings `inventory.barcode_generate_at` (sku_create default / grn / off) and `inventory.barcode_per_receipt_date` (no / yes); `PrepareItemBarcode` on Item create; `EnsureReceiptBarcodes` at GRN (permanent code unless off; one dated EAN-13 `02`+YYMMDD+NNNN per SKU per day in the new **ItemBarcode** register, daily `PIMDatedBarcodeSeq`); `ResolveItemBySKU` and pick/pack scans resolve registered codes; GRN stickers print the receipt's dated code. Tests `TestDatedEANFromParts`, `TestEnsureReceiptBarcodesPerDate` (caught a same-day second-receipt gap, fixed by recording every receipt on the register row) plus existing barcode/lookup/sticker suites green; live: an Item saved without a barcode was issued `0200000000011`. ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.3.1, USER_GUIDE §8.1.2.
- [x] **57.8 Non-sellable asset records.** *Decided by the user 2026-10-06:* **in the Fixed Assets module** (custody, depreciation), never in POS or sales. Build next. Whether this is a new
  Item type on the existing Item master or routes into the existing Asset module (Stage 55.16).
  The two are very different builds.
  — *2026-10-09: model approved by the user ("yes") and built + verified.* Model: `Item.item_type` = Stock | Fixed Asset; a Fixed Asset item is bought on a PO, and its GRN raises one Draft Asset per accepted unit in the existing Fixed Assets module instead of stock. - [x] **57.13 Two-factor recovery code count.** *Decided by the user 2026-10-07: keep 10. No change.* *[needs design decision: user — confirm intent]*
  Reported as "10 of 10 left — there should be no limit", but "10 of 10 left" is the count of
  *unused* single-use codes, not a cap that can be hit. `engines/mfa_recovery.go:28` sets
  `RecoveryCodeCount = 10` at 50 bits of entropy each; 10 is the industry norm and Stage 49.2.3
  deliberately requires single-use hashed codes, so "unlimited" is not a property recovery codes
  can have. If the real want is *regenerating* a fresh set on demand, that already exists and may
  only need to be more discoverable. **Do not change this without confirmation — it is a security
  control.**

### Documentation gaps this round exposed

  from the code, which makes the docs the defect: Sales Invoice vs Vendor Invoice (and the
  three-way match), Credit Note vs Debit Note (direction), POS Profile, webhook subscription (the
  transactional outbox), and rate limiting. Answers are drafted in the triage doc — fold them into
  `USER_GUIDE.md`/`ADMIN_GUIDE.md` rather than leaving them in a QA note.

- [x] **57.15 Resolve the Purchase Return contradiction.** *Decided by the user 2026-10-06:* **build it** - return-to-vendor against the original GRN, stock moves out, Debit Note raised automatically. The user asked "where is purchase
  return?" and the honest answer is that it does not exist: `docs/specs/modules_overview.md:55`
  and `PRD.md` §4.4 describe a Purchase Return/RTV module and `ERROR_CODES.md` reserves 5 error
  codes for it, but the doctype was never built (already recorded in archived Stage 25.4). Only
  the financial half exists, via DebitNote; the inventory half (scan against the original GRN,
  move stock to RTV Pending) is absent. **Either build it or correct the docs — shipping docs that
  promise a missing module is the worse of the two.** *[needs design decision: user — build or
  retract]*
  — *2026-10-05/06: USER_GUIDE §6.5 now states the truth (no Purchase Return screen; use a Debit Note with its Reference PO, which is now a real PO picker). The build-or-retract decision is still the user's.*
  — *2026-10-08: built and verified (user decision 2026-10-06: build it).* New doctype **PurchaseReturn** (migration `db/migrations_stage57_15_purchase_return.sql`, replayed into every tenant schema: doctype, fields, HR/Admin + Store Manager grants, `PRT` and `DN` number series). `engines/purchase_return.go`: a return is raised against the original GRN, capped per SKU/lot/bucket at what the GRN received (Accepted, or the QC Rejected/Damaged split) less posted returns and other open returns; a GRN that is Cancelled (its stock never posted) is refused. Validated at `ValidateTransactionalRules` (the generic doc API's choke point): PURCHA-0116 reason, 0117 over-return / line not on the GRN, 0118 no/cancelled GRN, 0119 already on a return; a doc-API write can only set Draft/Cancelled. `PostPurchaseReturn` (POST `/api/v1/procurement/purchase-returns/{id}/post`) in one transaction locks the return and the GRN, re-checks, moves accepted stock out of available via `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucherTx` (floor-checked) and set-aside stock out of qc_hold/damaged, posts Dr 5150 / Cr 1200, inserts the DebitNote, marks the return Posted; then posts the DebitNote through the unchanged `PostDebitNote` (Dr 2100 / Cr 5150; failure leaves it Draft with PURCHA-0120), writes stock-ledger entries and takes the receipt back out of item_cost. Maker-checker: if an approval rule exists for PurchaseReturn, only an Approved return posts. `GET .../purchase-returns/context` gives the returnable lines (scope-checked). **Found while verifying:** after a return, AP three-way match still valued the GRN at its gross accepted qty, so the vendor's correct net bill would hold and a bill for the returned units would match - `MatchVendorInvoice` now nets posted returns (engines/vendor_invoice.go). Screen: **Procurement → Purchase Return** (view-procurement.js). Tests: `TestPurchaseReturn` (all five codes' paths, stock 8/2 -> 5/0, DN posted, 2100/1200 net 500, 5150 nets 0, re-post refused, net bill matches, gross bill holds); server tests incl. module-manifest and route classification. Live on :8147: GRN `GRN/HO/26-27/000024` (8 accepted, 2 rejected at Rs 100) -> return 3 accepted + 2 rejected -> stock 5/5/0, `DN/HO/26-27/000001` Posted for 300, GL 2100 Cr 800 / Dr 300, 1200 Dr 800 / Cr 300, 5150 nets 0; browser: returned 2 of lot LOT-AUG on `GRN/HO/26-27/000022` (`DN/HO/26-27/000002`, the GRN then shows Returned 2 / Can return 3); approval-gated path (Draft post refused, self-approval refused, second user approves, post); Cancel releases the qty. Not done: GST input-credit reversal on returned goods (*done 2026-10-10, see "2026-10-09 five-item pass" item 4*), WMS bin quantities (stays out of erp-d1's wms files). USER_GUIDE §6.5 rewritten, ADMIN_GUIDE "Purchase Returns" section, `modules_overview.md` and PRD §4.4 corrected.

Also: the bill/receipt shows the store name only, not "Name (code)" (57.1); asset version 32 -> 33 (`app.js`/`index.html`, kept paired) because the last two deploys shipped with v=32 unchanged, so cached browsers could still run pre-Stage-57 screens. - [x] **57.20 FA-20261005-01 - AP three-way match math (2026-10-07).** `engines/vendor_invoice.go` `MatchVendorInvoice` (Match3Way now wraps it): expected bill = **accepted** qty (not received - rejected goods are not owed) x PO rate x the PO's own tax ratio (`grand_total/total_amount`); tolerance is measured against that, not the whole PO; overbilling still holds - this invoice plus every invoice already standing (Matched/Approved/Pending Approval/Paid) against the same GRN may not exceed its value, nor against the same PO its tax-inclusive total. PO/GRN are taken from the invoice itself (the UI no longer re-asks; a disagreeing reference is refused), and a hold returns plain reasons shown in the Match dialog (FA-06's "comparison details"). PO with no rates keeps the old rule (invoice = PO total), so it still fails closed. Tests: the audit's own fixture (100 @ 10 +18%, 80 received / 75 accepted: 885 matches, 944 holds), duplicate GRN bill holds, mismatched reference refused, plus all prior cases. Live: 1,180 full-receipt bill matched; a second 1,180 bill for the same GRN held with two reasons. USER_GUIDE §6.6 (new - there was no supplier-bill procedure). **Open:** GST input credit is still not split out of GRN Suspense at payment (whole gross clears 2100) - a CA-review item under 55.0F, not changed here.
- [x] **57.21 FA-20261005-03 - OMS order stuck at Reserved (2026-10-07).** Root cause: nothing in the product ever created a FulfillmentTask for an order - `CreateFulfillmentTasks` had only test callers since it was written. Built `ReleaseOrderToFulfillment` (engines/fulfillment.go) + `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/release-to-fulfillment` + a **Release to Fulfillment** button on a Reserved order's detail: one pick task per sourcing location (held lines excluded), order -> **Released**, in one transaction; idempotent (a repeat returns the same tasks). Once Released, Hold / Hold line / Switch Facility / Split / Cancel are refused by the shared `orderMutationAllowed` gate (StatusTransitionRule still overrides); `PlaceOrderHold` now goes through that gate too (it had none - a Shipped order could be put on hold). **Also found:** a **Cash on delivery** manual order was always held PAYMENT_PENDING and releasing the hold re-ran the same check - a dead end; COD now passes the payment gate. Test `TestReleaseOrderToFulfillment`; order/hold/cancel/fulfillment suites green; browser: Open -> Release to Fulfillment -> task listed on Fulfillment with Start Picking, no API errors. USER_GUIDE §9A.1/9A.3/9A.4. **Still open from FA-03:** pick -> ship -> notification -> invoice from the same order is not yet proven end to end; dispatch deducts `inventory_availability` directly with no stock-ledger/COGS posting (pre-existing, `TransitionTaskStatus`).
- [x] **57.22 Production outage: every page 403 on app.wholeops.in (reported by the user 2026-10-07, fixed same hour).** Root cause: since BLD-005, `deploy.ps1` uploads the frontend to `public.new` with `scp -r` as root, which lands as `root:root drwx------`; `remote_deploy.sh` moved it live without resetting ownership, so the `erp` service user could not read `public/` and the file server answered 403 for every page, while `/api/v1/health` stayed 200 and the deploy reported OK. At least the 02:49 (`c88b9e8`) and 03:41 (`480de79`) deploys shipped it (the rollback snapshot `public.prev` had the same mode). **Fixed on the box** (`chown -R erp:erp`, dirs 755 / files 644 on `public` and `public.prev`; site 200, v=33 served). **Fixed for every future deploy** in `deploy/remote_deploy.sh`: staged `public.new` is given to REMOTE_DIR's owner with readable modes before anything swaps, and `wait_healthy` now also requires `GET /` = 200 (not `/index.html`, which the Go file server 301s), so an unreadable frontend fails the deploy and auto-rolls back. `docs/qa/audit-deploy-mock.py` gains a `ui-forbidden` case (API 200, `/` 403) - 15/15 runs consistent, ui-forbidden rolls back; the chown/chmod sequence proven on the box against a root-owned 700 tree.
- [x] **57.23 GST Input Tax Credit on supplier bills, and output GST on credit sales (user decision 2026-10-07: "build standard ITC now").** Match records `taxable_amount`/`gst_amount`/`gst_split` at the PO's tax ratio; `inputGSTDebits` makes Pay and override-approved Pay post goods to 2100 and GST to 1500; Pay w/ TDS deducts TDS on the value excluding GST (CBDT Circular 23/2017) and also posts 1500. Order-level Sales Invoices now compute GST like package invoices (shared `resolveOrderInterstate`) and `PostSalesInvoice` books CGST/SGST/IGST to 2200/2201/2202 instead of revenue - credit sales' GST never reached the GST return before. GST Return Summary gains Input Tax Credit and Net GST Payable. Tests: ITC split (885 = 750 + 135), TDS 15 not 18, order invoice 236 = 200 + 18 + 18 posted to 1300/4100/2200/2201. Live: a 1,180 bill paid as 2100 1,000 / 1500 180 / cash 1,180; summary ITC 180. ADMIN_GUIDE (GST on purchases and credit sales), USER_GUIDE §6.6. **Still open:** CA review of eligibility (Section 17(5) blocked credits, reversals) - the system treats all input GST as claimable.
- [x] **57.24 Order shipping, end to end (FA-20261005-03 follow-through, 2026-10-08).** `TransitionTaskStatus` reads and locks the task inside its transaction and refuses a repeat Dispatched/Rejected (a second dispatch used to deduct stock again). Dispatched now ships qty minus short-picks, consumes the order line's reservation row (it stayed behind as a phantom the sweeper could release twice), marks the line Dispatched with `shipped_qty`, posts COGS (Dr 5100 / Cr 1200 at moving-average cost, standard cost fallback) in the same transaction and writes the stock ledger. Rejected re-reserves at the next node attributed to the order line (an unattributed row was dropped by the sweeper at TTL) and moves the line. Pick/pack saves no longer drop `line_id`; order closure ignores Rejected tasks (a rejected-then-rerouted order could never reach Shipped); the draft invoice bills what shipped. Tests `TestOrderShipEndToEnd` (Reserved → release → pick → pack → booking → manifest → handover → Shipped + draft invoice, stock 10→8, reservation gone, COGS 80, ledger -2, repeat refused), `TestOrderShipShortPick`, `TestOrderInvoiceBooksOutputGST`. USER_GUIDE §9A.4. **Not done:** bin-level stock is not decremented at dispatch (the Stage 59 bin-allocation work in another session covers it).

Credit Note's customer became a real Link (`69ded6b`); Bin picker shows bin codes (`f891ace`); offer "Applies To" label (`46b751a`); Purchase Return names vendor and location (`680d1f0`); pickers lead with the name (`6261c5b`); records named by `label`/`title` show it (`f7c8789`); PIM Workbench Family column and detail heading (`a5fefdf` - the family's series prefix "Design" is not a record type, so the general sweep skipped it). *Dead ends:* no screen could create a journal voucher - **Financial Accounting → Journal Vouchers** (`691f5b7`); asset Transfer/Dispose were typed-code prompts - now a name-picker panel (`61c6f2b`); Quality Inspection Submit and other procurement-owned row buttons did nothing until Procurement had been opened (`fbdca9d`); **Service Ticket's lifecycle (Stage 37.8) had routes but no button** - rows now offer Assign / Start / Resolve / Close / Cancel through the dedicated endpoints (`971a275`). *Reports:* Stock Ledger's Running Balance was 0 on every row unless both SKU and Location were filtered - now each item/location card's true balance, summed in SQL before the voucher/date filters (`040455a`, test `StockLedgerBalancePerCardWithoutFilters`); catalog cells holding a timestamp read as a local date and time. *Pickers:* `attachTypeahead` stacked a second picker each time a screen re-attached it (Sticker Printing's Module switch) - the stale one searched the old record type and its menu covered the new one; re-attaching now aborts the earlier listeners (`dc4e365`). *Background workers (found tracing why the birthday campaign might not send):* `listTenantSchemas` checked module entitlements with the schema name, which `GetTenantSchema` resolves to tenant_default - so all 15 module-owned workers (campaigns, loyalty, OMS/Magento/Unicommerce sync, PIM import/export/publish, reports, WMS waves...) followed tenant_default's switches for every tenant, prod `tenant_minn` included. Now the registry's tenant id goes with each schema (`37b105b`, test `TestWorkerEligibilityFollowsEachTenantsOwnSwitch`, fails on the old code). *Generic create (found recording `bom_create`):* recreating a deleted record's code answered 200 but left it deleted and invisible - it now comes back with the new data; and a create whose id belonged to another record type overwrote that record - now 409 (`7657453`, `TestGenericCreateUpsertGuards`, fails on the old handler for both).
